## BASIC LEARNING SESSION

### Exact printed ownership and cross-topic firewall

The printed clause owns **alethic, epistemic and soteriological theories of religious diversity**, the problem of absolute truth, and their implications for dialogue, tolerance and coexistence.

| Owned here | Boundary that is not crossed |
|---|---|
| exclusivism, inclusivism, pluralism and the particularist replies to them | complete concepts of God belong to the Notions of God owner |
| conflicting revelation and conflicting experience **as evidence of diversity** | authenticating a revelation, and testing an experience for veridicality, belong to their own owners |
| Hick's Real and its humanly conditioned manifestations | the complete theory of religious experience, and the complete semantics of God-talk, belong to those owners |
| tolerance, religious freedom and dialogue **only where a printed question routes them here** | full secularism, multiculturalism and political-rights theory belong to Socio-Political Philosophy |
| Indian many-sidedness, many-paths and equal-respect arguments **as pluralism resources** | complete Jain, Vedāntic and individual-thinker doctrine belongs to the Paper I owners |
| the missionary and identity costs that pluralist theories impose on traditions | descriptive history and evaluation of particular present-day communities are outside scope |

⚠️ **[FACT]** The firewall that governs the whole clause: **social tolerance, epistemic humility, alethic pluralism and soteriological pluralism are four different theses.** Peaceful coexistence neither proves that rival claims are true nor requires treating every religion as equally true. Almost every weak script on this clause slides from "we should live together peacefully" to "therefore all religions are equally true", and never recovers.

### 0A. FOUR AXES THAT MUST NOT BE MERGED

```text
        MANY RELIGIONS, CONFLICTING TRUTH-CLAIMS — FOUR SEPARATE QUESTIONS
  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────┬───────────────────┐
    1. TRUTH        2. SALVATION       3. ACCESS          4. COEXISTENCE
    (alethic)       (soteriological)   (epistemic)        (political)
    which claims    can another        what should        how should
    are true,       tradition really   disagreement do    citizens and
    false,          transform or       to my level of     institutions
    symbolic,       liberate its       confidence?        treat deep
    partly          adherents?                            disagreement?
    adequate?
  ──────────────  ──────────────────  ──────────────────  ───────────────────
    one Real +      several            fallibilism,        freedom,
    non-exhaustive  efficacious        humility,           reciprocity,
    formulations;   paths, possibly    steadfast           respect,
    or several      toward DIFFERENT   particularism,      non-coercion —
    irreducible     ends               or suspension       WITHOUT agreement
    truths/ends                                            about truth
  └──────────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────┴───────────────────┘
        ↓ THE TWO ILLICIT SLIDES THAT COST THE MOST MARKS ↓
   "we coexist peacefully"  ==>  "all religions are equally true"   ✗ (4 → 1)
   "I hold the absolute truth" ==> "I must be an exclusivist"       ✗ (1 → 1)
```

*Read the four columns as four independent questions. A position may be pluralist on one axis and exclusivist on another, which is why the axis must be named before any thinker is discussed.*

> 🔑 **Mnemonic — "Truth · Salvation · Access · Coexistence" (T-SAC):** name the axis in your first sentence. Positions differ across these four axes **independently**, and a victory on one concedes nothing on the others.

**[FACT] Pluralism is not relativism.** A pluralist can deny that all propositions are equally true, can retain the law of non-contradiction, and can still reject any one tradition's claim to exhaustive possession of the absolute. **[FACT] Conversely, tolerance is a civic ethic and can be defended by an exclusivist** — Locke's argument that force cannot produce belief is available to someone who thinks every other religion false.

### Local source and factual control

- Local searchable John Hick, *Philosophy of Religion*, print pp. 109–119 (PDF pp. 120–130), controls the treatment of conflicting truth-claims and the philosophical framework for pluralism.
- The local searchable *Oxford Handbook of Philosophy of Religion*, "Religious Diversity," print pp. 392–417 (PDF pp. 404–429), controls competing mystical practices, toleration and comparative-philosophy pressures.
- Local searchable Chatterjee–Datta, especially PDF pp. 109–118, controls Jain many-sidedness and conditional predication; local Radhakrishnan is used only for attributed experiential-unity claims, and never as proof that all religious ends coincide.
- **[FACT]** No survey of religious demography, no conflict casualty count and no contemporary communal statistic is used anywhere in this package to settle a philosophical question. Where historical or behavioural material appears, it is used to test a *claim about causation*, never to establish what is true.

### Origin, chronology and anachronism control

A calendar timeline is not the natural shape of this clause: it is a dispute about *how to respond to disagreement*, not an institution with a founding date. What is required is the minimum chronology that prevents anachronism, plus the dated print anchors an examiner can be quoted at.

```text
CONCEPTUAL ORIGIN OF THE PROBLEM
  Stage 1  A tradition states determinate truth-claims and a way of salvation.
  Stage 2  It meets other traditions doing the same thing, incompatibly.
           ==> the problem is not diversity as such; it is diversity
               plus determinate claims plus a demand for consistency.
  Stage 3  First response - EXCLUSIVISM: one tradition is decisively true;
           the rest are, to that extent, mistaken.
  Stage 4  Second response - INCLUSIVISM: the others are partly true and are
           fulfilled in mine; their adherents may be saved through my truth.
  Stage 5  Third response - PLURALISM: several traditions are authentic
           responses to one ultimate Reality (Hick), or attain genuinely
           DIFFERENT real ends (Heim).
  Stage 6  The counter-question: does holding ONE absolute truth make
           exclusivism, and therefore conflict, INEVITABLE?
           ==> this is what 2021, 2022 and 2024 print.
```

### SESSION 1 — FOUR QUESTIONS HIDING INSIDE ONE: WHICH AXIS IS THE STEM ASKING ABOUT?

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** "Religious pluralism" can mean four completely different things: that several religions are *true*; that several religions *save or liberate*; that no one is entitled to *certainty* about which is right; or that a state must let people *live together* without settling which is right. These are four claims, not four wordings of one claim.

**Technical definition.** The clause tests four separable relations. **Alethic** pluralism concerns the truth-value of religious propositions. **Soteriological** pluralism concerns the efficacy of traditions in producing salvation, liberation or transformation. **Epistemic** pluralism concerns what awareness of disagreement should do to a believer's confidence. **Political** pluralism concerns the terms on which citizens and institutions handle deep religious disagreement. The problem of **absolute truth** is a fifth question that cuts across all four: whether affirming one absolute truth commits one to exclusivism on any of them.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Religious diversity is not by itself a philosophical problem; the problem begins when determinate truth-claims, a determinate way of salvation and a demand for consistency are put together — and since truth, salvation, epistemic access and political coexistence then come apart into four independent questions, an answer that has not said which of them it is discussing has not yet begun.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**alethic · soteriological · epistemic access · political coexistence · conflicting truth-claims · determinate claim · non-contradiction · exhaustive possession · perspectival access · fallibilism**

**How to use them:** open every answer in this clause by naming the axis the stem asserts. The single sentence "truth, salvation, epistemic access and coexistence are four questions, not one" converts an average script into a good one before any thinker has been named.

#### VISUAL — THE FOUR AXES, AND THE TWO ILLICIT SLIDES

```text
 ┌────────────────── WHY DIVERSITY BECOMES A PROBLEM ──────────────────┐
 │  determinate truth-claims  +  determinate salvation  +  consistency │
 │        ↓                          ↓                        ↓        │
 │  "God is personal"          "salvation is through"   both cannot    │
 │  vs "the ultimate is        vs "liberation is by     be true in the │
 │  beyond personality"        insight into no-self"    same respect    │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                   ↓
   AXIS 1 TRUTH       AXIS 2 SALVATION    AXIS 3 ACCESS      AXIS 4 COEXISTENCE
   which claims       can another         what should        how do we live
   are true?          path liberate?      disagreement do    together without
                                          to confidence?     agreeing?
   ───────────        ───────────         ───────────        ───────────
   exclusivist:       exclusivist:        steadfast:         exclusivist CAN
   mine true          only mine saves     hold firm          still be tolerant
   inclusivist:       inclusivist:        conciliationist:   pluralist CAN
   mine fullest       others saved        lower confidence   still be coercive
   pluralist:         through mine        fallibilist:       (nothing follows
   several            pluralist:          hold, but          automatically in
   authentic          several             corrigibly         either direction)
                      efficacious
   ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   SLIDE A (4 → 1):  "we coexist"  ⇒  "all are equally true"      ✗
   SLIDE B (1 → 1):  "there is one absolute truth"
                        ⇒  "therefore I am an exclusivist"        ✗
   Both slides need a suppressed premise. Naming it is the answer.
```

*The top box shows why diversity generates a problem at all; the four columns show that positions vary across the axes independently; the bottom strip names the two invalid inferences the whole clause exists to block.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] Diversity alone is not the problem.** Religions differ in ritual, calendar, language and art without any philosophical difficulty arising. The problem arises only when three things hold together: traditions make **determinate** claims about what is ultimately real; they attach **salvation, liberation or release** to a determinate way; and we accept that contradictory claims cannot both be true in the same respect. Remove any one and the problem dissolves — which is why some pluralisms try to remove one.

**[FACT] Axis 1 — truth (alethic).** Which doctrines are true, false, symbolic or partly adequate? A pluralist answer on this axis says either that there is one ultimate Reality with several non-exhaustive human formulations, or that there are several irreducible religious truths and ends. It does **not** say that every proposition is equally true.

**[FACT] Axis 2 — salvation and liberation (soteriological).** Can other traditions genuinely transform or save? A pluralist answer says several paths are efficacious — and, in one important version, that they are efficacious toward **different ends**. This axis is separable from truth: one could hold that another tradition's doctrines are largely false while its practice still transforms, and one could hold the reverse.

**[FACT] Axis 3 — epistemic access.** What should the discovery of disagreement do to my confidence? The available answers are fallibilism, intellectual humility, steadfast particularism and suspension of judgement. Session 16 shows that this axis has its own literature and its own verdict, and that the verdict does not settle axis 1.

**[FACT] Axis 4 — political coexistence.** How should citizens and institutions treat disagreement? The pluralist answers here are freedom, reciprocity, respect and non-coercion — **without** requiring agreement about truth. This is why toleration can be argued by an exclusivist, and why a pluralist metaphysics does not by itself deliver a political conclusion.

**[FACT] Pluralism is not relativism.** The pluralist can deny that all propositions are equally true, retain non-contradiction, and still reject the claim that one finite tradition exhaustively possesses the absolute. Relativism denies that there is one truth of the matter; pluralism typically affirms it and denies that any finite formulation exhausts it. Collapsing the two is the commonest single error in this clause.

**[INFERENCE] Why the separation is load-bearing.** Because each axis has its own alternatives, refuting soteriological pluralism leaves alethic pluralism untouched, and demonstrating that coexistence is possible leaves both untouched. The whole examinable value of the clause lies in refusing to let a victory on one axis count as a victory on another.

**Objection.** This looks like scholastic tidiness. The believer does not experience truth, salvation, access and coexistence as four separate things; she experiences one faith.
**Reply.** **[INFERENCE]** Conceded, and the concession should be made in the answer, because it is true and it shows judgement. But a *philosophical* question about diversity asks what would survive the removal of what — and that can only be answered by pulling the lived unity apart. The lived fusion is evidence about religious psychology; it is not an argument that the four axes are one.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** "Religious pluralism" is ambiguous between four separable theses, and a stem must be assigned to one of them before it can be assessed.
> **Named evidence:** The alethic thesis concerns the truth of doctrines; the soteriological thesis concerns whether another path liberates, and Heim's *Salvations* holds that the ends themselves differ; the epistemic thesis is the subject of the peer- and deep-disagreement literature from Fogelin onward; the political thesis is the subject of Locke, Mill, Rawls, Forst, Aśoka's Rock Edict XII and Bhargava's principled distance.
> **Analysis:** Because each axis has its own alternatives and its own literature, an argument that succeeds on one leaves the others standing — which is why "religions coexist peacefully" is no evidence that their doctrines are jointly true, and why "there is one absolute Truth" does not by itself entail that one tradition monopolises it.
> **Qualification:** The separation is analytic rather than phenomenological; in lived faith the four are fused, and an answer that concedes the fusion while insisting on the analytic distinction is stronger than one that pretends the believer experiences four things.

#### UPSC TRAPS

1. ⚠️ Do not define pluralism as "all religions say the same thing." They demonstrably do not, and the definition concedes the game to the exclusivist in the first line.
2. ⚠️ Do not infer political tolerance directly from a metaphysical thesis without supplying the argument. The inference needs a premise about coercion, harm or reciprocity, and that premise is where the marks are.
3. ⚠️ Do not treat "pluralism" as a single position. Hick's identist pluralism and Heim's differential pluralism give **incompatible** answers on axes 1 and 2, and a script that merges them cannot state either correctly.

#### MINI RECAP

Four axes — truth, salvation, access, coexistence. Two illicit slides — coexistence to equal truth, absolute truth to exclusivism. One habit — name the axis in your first sentence and never let a win on one count as a win on another.

---

### SESSION 2 — EXCLUSIVISM STATED PRECISELY, AND CITED CORRECTLY

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** Exclusivism is the view that one tradition or one revelation has *decisively* got it right — so that where others contradict it they are, to that extent, mistaken — and, in its soteriological form, that salvation is available through that tradition alone.

**Technical definition.** Exclusivism asserts that one tradition's revelation possesses uniquely decisive **alethic** authority, **soteriological** authority, or both. It appeals to a determinate revelation and to the principle of non-contradiction. It does **not** entail coercion, hostility or a denial of civic tolerance: an exclusivist may defend religious freedom vigorously while denying that other religions are equally true.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Exclusivism is not the same thing as intolerance, and treating the two as synonyms is the fastest way to lose the argument: the exclusivist's claim is that determinate revelation plus non-contradiction leaves no room for equal truth, and that claim has to be met on its merits rather than by attributing bad character to the person holding it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**decisive revelation · non-contradiction · *extra ecclesiam nulla salus* · Cyprian of Carthage · *salus extra ecclesiam non est* · Lateran IV (1215) · *Unam Sanctam* (1302) · Council of Florence (1442) · *Lumen Gentium* 16 · *Nostra Aetate* (1965) · privileged warrant · arbitrariness objection · contingency of birth**

**How to use them:** the attribution chain is worth more than any adjective. A script that gets Cyprian's own wording right, dates the conciliar hardening, and then notes the Vatican II qualification has demonstrated in three sentences that it knows the material.

#### VISUAL — THE ATTRIBUTION CHAIN THAT EXAMINERS CHECK

```text
  CYPRIAN OF CARTHAGE (d. 258)
  ├─ Epistle 73:  "salus extra ecclesiam non est"
  │               = "there is no salvation outside the Church"   ← HIS wording
  └─ De Ecclesiae Catholicae Unitate 6: one cannot have God as
     Father who does not have the Church as mother
                    │
                    │  compression into the lapidary tag
                    ↓
  "EXTRA ECCLESIAM NULLA SALUS"   ← LATER STANDARD PHRASING
                    │
     conciliar hardening ──┬── 1215  Fourth Lateran Council
                           ├── 1302  Unam Sanctam
                           └── 1442  Council of Florence
                    │
                    ↓
  1965  VATICAN II  ──  Lumen Gentium 16 · Nostra Aetate
        ==> the same tradition moves DECISIVELY toward INCLUSIVISM
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  ✗ WRONG: "extra ecclesiam nulla salus is the Christian position"
  ✓ RIGHT: it is a later standard formula, traced to Cyprian, hardened
           by three councils, and heavily qualified in 1965
```

*Follow the arrows downward: the wording changes, the authority hardens, and then the tradition itself qualifies the doctrine. Citing only the middle of this chain is the factual error the trap list names.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] The doctrine statement.** Exclusivism makes one tradition's decisive truth or salvation claim uniquely valid. Its argument appeals to two things: a **determinate revelation**, which is supposed to settle the content, and **non-contradiction**, which forbids affirming incompatible claims together.

**[FACT] Its presupposition.** One revelation has **privileged warrant**, and salvation can be specified independently enough for traditions to be compared on it. Both halves are contestable, and naming the presupposition is what separates a good script from an average one.

**[FACT] The Latin tag, handled correctly.** The lapidary formula is ***extra ecclesiam nulla salus*** — "outside the Church there is no salvation". The doctrine is standardly traced to **Cyprian of Carthage** (d. 258), whose own wording in *Epistle* 73 is *salus extra ecclesiam non est*; he also writes in *De Ecclesiae Catholicae Unitate* 6 that one cannot have God as Father who does not have the Church as mother. The compressed formula is the **later standard phrasing**, given conciliar form in the medieval period — Lateran IV (1215), *Unam Sanctam* (1302), the Council of Florence (1442).

**[FACT] The qualification that must not be omitted.** The same tradition later qualified the doctrine heavily. Vatican II's *Lumen Gentium* 16 and *Nostra Aetate* (1965) move the Roman Catholic position decisively toward **inclusivism**. Citing the tag as the settled Christian view is therefore a **factual error**, not merely an uncharitable reading.

**[FACT] Strength.** Exclusivism takes determinate truth seriously. It refuses to purchase harmony by draining the claims of content, and it respects the traditions' own self-understanding better than any theory that redescribes them as culturally limited appearances.

**[FACT] Weakness.** Two pressures. The **privileged-warrant** pressure: why should this revelation, rather than a rival one, carry decisive authority? The **arbitrariness** pressure, in its sharpest form the **contingency of birth**: one's tradition is overwhelmingly predicted by where one was born, which looks like a poor basis for a claim to unique access.

**[FACT] Exclusivism does not entail coercion.** An exclusivist may hold every other religion false and still defend religious freedom — on Locke's ground that force cannot produce belief, on the autonomy ground, or on the epistemic ground. The inference "exclusivist, therefore intolerant" needs an extra premise about what one may do to those one believes mistaken, and that premise is exactly what Locke denies.

**Objection.** Exclusivism is arrogant: it claims for one tradition what it denies to every other, on evidence no better than theirs.
**Reply.** **[FACT]** This is the objection Plantinga answers directly, and Session 8 states his three replies in full. In outline: believing *p* while knowing that others deny *p* is the ordinary condition of every philosopher, including the pluralist, so if it is arrogance here it is arrogance everywhere. **[INFERENCE]** The reply establishes *permissibility*, not truth — and it does not touch the contingency-of-birth problem, which is the objection that actually bites.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** Exclusivism is the thesis that one revelation carries uniquely decisive truth or salvific authority, and it is a position about warrant rather than about tolerance.
> **Named evidence:** Its classical soteriological expression is *extra ecclesiam nulla salus* — a later standard phrasing of Cyprian's *salus extra ecclesiam non est* (*Epistle* 73), hardened at Lateran IV in 1215, in *Unam Sanctam* in 1302 and at Florence in 1442, and then decisively qualified by *Lumen Gentium* 16 and *Nostra Aetate* in 1965.
> **Analysis:** The position's strength is that it preserves determinate content and non-contradiction; its weakness is that it must explain why *this* revelation carries privileged warrant when the believer's tradition is so strongly predicted by birth.
> **Qualification:** Exclusivism about truth does not entail intolerance in practice — Locke's argument that force cannot produce belief is fully available to an exclusivist — so the position must be refuted on warrant, not on manners.

#### UPSC TRAPS

4. ⚠️ Do not present exclusivism only as bigotry. The examiner is testing whether you can state a position you reject at its strongest, and the graded ladder puts "exclusivism presented only as bigotry" in the average band.
5. ⚠️ Do not quote ***extra ecclesiam nulla salus*** as Cyprian's own words. His wording is *salus extra ecclesiam non est* (*Ep.* 73); the compressed tag is the later standard formula.
6. ⚠️ Do not present the tag as the settled Christian position. *Lumen Gentium* 16 and *Nostra Aetate* (1965) moved Roman Catholic teaching decisively toward inclusivism, and omitting that is a factual error an examiner can mark down.

#### MINI RECAP

One decisive revelation; determinate truth plus non-contradiction; the tag is Cyprian-derived but later, hardened by three councils, qualified in 1965. Strength: content preserved. Weakness: privileged warrant and the contingency of birth. And exclusivism about truth is not intolerance about practice.

---

### SESSION 3 — INCLUSIVISM: GENEROUS, AND STILL NOT PLURALISM

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** Inclusivism says: my religion has the fullest truth, but other religions contain real, partial truth, and their sincere adherents may be saved — through what my religion holds to be the operative reality, even if they do not describe it that way.

**Technical definition.** Inclusivism grants truth or salvation beyond the home tradition while interpreting that success **through** the home tradition's categories. It preserves the home tradition's normative priority and adds the universality of divine grace. Karl Rahner's *anonymous Christians* is the standard model: the person of another faith who responds to grace is, on this account, already related to the reality the home tradition names.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Inclusivism is the position that concedes most and moves least: it grants that other traditions are genuinely true and genuinely saving, and then explains that success in terms the other tradition would not accept — which is why the decisive question about it is not whether it is generous, but on whose terms the generosity is extended.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**partial truth · fulfilment · normative priority · anonymous Christians · Karl Rahner · universality of grace · home-tradition norm · redescription · patronising objection · inclusivism ≠ pluralism**

**How to use them:** the phrase that earns the mark is "**others are included on terms set by the privileged tradition**". Write it, and the difference from pluralism has been stated exactly.

#### VISUAL — WHERE INCLUSIVISM SITS, AND WHY IT IS NOT PLURALISM

```text
                    WHO SETS THE TERMS OF THE JUDGEMENT?
  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────┬────────────────────────┐
    EXCLUSIVISM         INCLUSIVISM             PLURALISM
    my tradition        my tradition            NO first-order
    sets the terms      sets the terms          tradition sets them;
    AND excludes        AND includes            a second-order
    the others          the others              account does
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
    others: false       others: PARTIALLY       others: authentic
                        true, FULFILLED         responses in their
                        in mine                 own right
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
    salvation:          salvation: possible     salvation: several
    only here           elsewhere, but          efficacious paths
                        THROUGH my reality      (Hick), or several
                        (Rahner: "anonymous     different ENDS
                        Christians")            (Heim)
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────┴────────────────────────┘
        ↑                       ↑                        ↑
   privileged            STILL privileged        privilege relocated
   warrant               warrant, softened       to the theory itself
                         at the edges            → D'Costa's charge
                                                   (see Session 8)
```

*The middle column is the whole point: inclusivism moves the boundary of who is saved without moving the location of the standard. That is why it is a distinct third position and not a mild pluralism.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] The doctrine statement.** Inclusivism grants truth or salvation beyond the home tradition while interpreting that success through the home tradition. It is a **two-part** claim: an alethic part (other traditions contain partial truth) and a soteriological part (their adherents may be saved).

**[FACT] The argument.** It shares the exclusivist's appeal to determinate revelation, and adds the **universality of divine grace**: a good and universally concerned God would not confine salvation to those who happened to hear a particular message. Retaining normative priority is what keeps it from becoming pluralism.

**[FACT] The presupposition.** As with exclusivism, one revelation has **privileged warrant**; what changes is the *scope* of grace, not the *location* of the standard.

**[FACT] The distinction that must be written.** Inclusivism is **not** pluralism: others are included on terms set by the privileged tradition. Pluralism, by contrast, refuses to let any first-order tradition supply the adjudicating framework — which, as Session 8 shows, generates its own problem.

**[FACT] The canonical example.** Rahner's **"anonymous Christian"** is the standard inclusivist model. **[INFERENCE]** Note what it does and does not say: it does not say that the other person is secretly a member of an institution; it says that the grace to which she responds is, on the home tradition's account, the same grace it names differently.

**[FACT] The Vatican II link, which ties Sessions 2 and 3 together.** *Lumen Gentium* 16 and *Nostra Aetate* (1965) are precisely the documents by which a tradition that had hardened the exclusivist tag moved toward inclusivism. The same historical chain therefore illustrates both positions, and citing it in this order shows the examiner that you have understood the movement rather than memorised two labels.

**Objection.** Inclusivism is patronising: it judges others by a standard they have not accepted and redescribes their faith in categories they reject.
**Reply.** **[FACT]** Every judgement uses some framework; there is no view from nowhere from which traditions could be assessed. **[INFERENCE]** So the ethical issue is not *whether* a framework is used but whether it remains **open to correction** and whether it **respects other self-descriptions** — that is, whether the inclusivist is willing to hear the other tradition say "that is not what I am doing" and treat the objection as data.

**[INFERENCE] The examinable sting.** The reply defends inclusivism against the charge of *arbitrary* condescension but not against the charge of **asymmetry**: the home tradition is never redescribed in the other's categories. An answer that names that asymmetry has found the position's real cost.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** Inclusivism concedes real truth and real salvific efficacy to other traditions while retaining its own tradition's normative priority, and is therefore a third position rather than a softened exclusivism or an incipient pluralism.
> **Named evidence:** Rahner's "anonymous Christians" is the standard model, and *Lumen Gentium* 16 together with *Nostra Aetate* (1965) marks the institutional movement of a tradition from the *extra ecclesiam* formula toward exactly this position.
> **Analysis:** The move extends the scope of grace without relocating the standard, so other traditions are included **on terms they have not set**; that is what distinguishes it from pluralism, and it is also the source of the patronising objection.
> **Qualification:** The objection is answerable in part — every judgement uses some framework — but the asymmetry remains, since the home tradition is never itself redescribed in the other's categories, and an honest inclusivism should concede that cost rather than deny it.

#### UPSC TRAPS

7. ⚠️ Do not confuse inclusivism with pluralism. This is trap 2 in the owner's list and it is fatal in the 2018 Q6(a) "distinguish" question, where the whole mark scheme is the three-way differentia.
8. ⚠️ Do not describe "anonymous Christians" as a claim about secret institutional membership. It is a claim about the operation of grace, and the caricature makes the position look sillier than it is.
9. ⚠️ Do not present the patronising objection as decisive without stating the reply. The strongest version of the critique is the **asymmetry** — the home tradition is never redescribed — not the mere fact that a framework is used.

#### MINI RECAP

Partial truth plus possible salvation, both interpreted through the home tradition. Rahner is the model; Vatican II is the institutional instance. The differentia from pluralism is one sentence: *others are included on terms set by the privileged tradition.* The residual cost is asymmetry.

---

### SESSION 4 — PLURALISM DEFINED, AND THE FOUR THINGS IT IS NOT

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** Pluralism says that several religious traditions are genuine, authentic responses to what is ultimately real — not that they say the same thing, not that every doctrine is equally true, and not that truth does not matter.

**Technical definition.** Pluralism holds that several traditions may be authentic responses to ultimate reality, or may realise genuinely different religious ends. It does **not** follow that every doctrine, practice or truth-claim is equally valid. John Hick's version treats the great post-axial traditions as culturally conditioned responses to one ultimate Reality (**"the Real"**), assessed by transformation from self-centredness to Reality-centredness.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Pluralism is defined by what it denies, not by what it affirms about harmony: it denies that any one finite tradition exhaustively possesses the absolute, and that denial is compatible with holding that some religious claims are simply false — which is why "all religions are the same" is not a statement of pluralism but a caricature of it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**authentic response · ultimate Reality · the Real · culturally conditioned · post-axial traditions · non-exhaustive formulation · exhaustive possession denied · not relativism · not indifferentism · not syncretism · not perennialist identity**

**How to use them:** define pluralism by the denial — "no finite tradition exhaustively possesses the absolute" — and then immediately fence off the four confusions below. Two sentences, and the answer has already avoided the weak band.

#### VISUAL — THE FENCE AROUND THE DEFINITION

```text
   ┌──────────────────── PLURALISM AFFIRMS ────────────────────┐
   │  several traditions are AUTHENTIC responses to ultimate   │
   │  reality, OR realise genuinely DIFFERENT religious ends   │
   │  → no finite tradition EXHAUSTIVELY possesses the absolute│
   └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        ✗                ✗                ✗                ✗
   ┌─────────┐     ┌───────────┐    ┌────────────┐   ┌────────────┐
   │RELATIVISM│     │INDIFFEREN-│    │ SYNCRETISM │   │PERENNIALIST│
   │no truth  │     │TISM       │    │ blend into │   │IDENTITY    │
   │of the    │     │truth does │    │ one new    │   │"all say the│
   │matter    │     │not matter │    │ religion   │   │same thing" │
   ├─────────┤     ├───────────┤    ├────────────┤   ├────────────┤
   │Pluralism│     │Pluralism  │    │Pluralism   │   │Pluralism   │
   │AFFIRMS a│     │makes truth│    │keeps the   │   │explains WHY│
   │truth of │     │the whole  │    │traditions  │   │they differ,│
   │the      │     │point — it │    │DISTINCT    │   │it does not │
   │matter   │     │relocates  │    │            │   │deny that   │
   │         │     │it         │    │            │   │they do     │
   └─────────┘     └───────────┘    └────────────┘   └────────────┘
   ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   THE RESIDUE PLURALISM MUST STILL FACE (Session 15):
   a personal creator cannot both exist and not exist in the same
   respect; the self cannot be both permanent and denied in the
   same sense. Level, symbol, standpoint and end may be reinterpreted
   — a remaining contradiction must be ACKNOWLEDGED, not dissolved.
```

*The top box is the definition; the four crossed boxes are the confusions; the bottom strip is the honest residue that separates a top-band answer from a merely fluent one.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] The core affirmation.** Several traditions may be authentic responses to ultimate reality, or may realise genuinely different religious ends. Hick's version, which Sessions 5–7 develop, treats the great post-axial traditions as culturally conditioned responses to one ultimate Reality assessed soteriologically.

**[FACT] The core denial.** It does **not** follow that every doctrine, practice or truth-claim is equally valid. This sentence is in the owner file and it is the sentence that most scripts omit.

**[INFERENCE] Confusion 1 — relativism.** Relativism denies that there is a truth of the matter. Pluralism typically **affirms** an ultimate reality and denies that any finite formulation exhausts it. The difference is between "there is nothing to be right about" and "there is something to be right about, and no one of us has it whole."

**[INFERENCE] Confusion 2 — indifferentism.** Indifferentism says the question does not matter. Pluralism says it matters supremely, which is why it constructs an elaborate second-order theory rather than shrugging.

**[INFERENCE] Confusion 3 — syncretism.** Syncretism blends traditions into a new one. Pluralism, in every serious version, keeps the traditions distinct: Hick because the conceptual schemes really differ, Heim because the **ends** really differ.

**[INFERENCE] Confusion 4 — perennialist identity.** "All religions say the same thing" is the caricature. Hick's whole apparatus exists to explain **why they differ** while remaining responses to the same Real; if they said the same thing he would need no theory at all.

**[FACT] Its acknowledged weakness.** Pluralism seems to relativise or dilute each religion's specific claims, and the "**Real *an sich***" — the Real as it is in itself — is unknowable. Session 6 states this objection in its technical form and gives the reply.

**[FACT] The relativism criticism and its reply.** *Criticism:* equal validity erases truth. *Reply:* distinguish **equal dignity** or **salvific efficacy** from **identical propositional truth**. A pluralist can accord every tradition equal dignity and equal civic standing without saying their propositions are equally true.

**[FACT] The self-reference criticism and its reply.** *Criticism:* pluralism itself becomes an exclusive meta-truth. *Reply:* hold it as a defeasible hypothesis rather than a creed. *Rejoinder:* it still judges first-order traditions — and Session 8 shows that this rejoinder, in D'Costa's hands, is the strongest objection in the field.

**Objection.** If pluralism must concede that some doctrines are false, how is it different from a mild inclusivism?
**Reply.** **[INFERENCE]** By where the standard sits. The inclusivist judges other traditions by *his own first-order tradition*; the pluralist judges by a *second-order* account that claims to privilege no first-order tradition. Whether that claim can be sustained is precisely what D'Costa denies — so the difference is real, but it is the difference that generates pluralism's own deepest problem.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** Pluralism holds that several traditions are authentic responses to ultimate reality, or attain genuinely different religious ends, and it is therefore defined by the denial that any finite tradition exhaustively possesses the absolute.
> **Named evidence:** Hick treats the post-axial traditions as culturally conditioned responses to the Real, assessed by transformation from self-centredness to Reality-centredness; Heim's *Salvations* (1995) holds instead that the ends themselves are plural.
> **Analysis:** So defined, pluralism is distinct from relativism (which denies a truth of the matter), from indifferentism (which denies that it matters), from syncretism (which merges the traditions) and from perennialist identity (which denies that they really differ) — and it must still concede that some doctrines are false.
> **Qualification:** Its residual difficulty is that the Real *an sich* is unknowable and that the theory redescribes traditions against their self-understanding, which is why the strongest form of the objection is not relativism but D'Costa's charge that pluralism is a covert exclusivism.

#### UPSC TRAPS

10. ⚠️ Do not define pluralism as "all religions lead to the same God". The verdict ladder places that phrase in the weak band by itself.
11. ⚠️ Do not answer the relativism charge by asserting harmony. Answer it by distinguishing **equal dignity and salvific efficacy** from **identical propositional truth** — that is the reply the owner records.
12. ⚠️ Do not forget that pluralism must concede that some claims are false. A pluralism that concedes nothing has become the caricature its critics attack.

#### MINI RECAP

Affirms: several authentic responses, or several real ends. Denies: exhaustive possession by any finite tradition. Not relativism, not indifferentism, not syncretism, not "all say the same thing." Residual cost: the unknowable Real and the redescription of traditions — which is where D'Costa waits.

---
### SESSION 5 — HICK I: THE COPERNICAN REVOLUTION AND THE ARGUMENT FOR IT

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** Hick's proposal is that we stop putting one religion — or one incarnation — at the centre of the religious universe and put ultimate Reality there instead, with the traditions in orbit around it. That is why he calls it a Copernican revolution.

**Technical definition.** Hick's **pluralistic hypothesis** holds that the major traditions are culturally conditioned human responses to the transcategorial **Real**, and are assessed **soteriologically** by the transformation they produce from self-centredness to Reality-centredness. It is offered as a **second-order philosophical hypothesis** about the traditions, not as a new first-order religion.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Hick's move is structural rather than devotional: he does not argue that any particular doctrine is false, he argues that the centre of the system has been misplaced — and once the Real rather than a tradition occupies the centre, the persistence of several transforming traditions stops being an anomaly to be explained away and becomes exactly what the hypothesis predicts.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**Copernican revolution · the Real · transcategorial · culturally conditioned response · post-axial traditions · second-order hypothesis · contingency of birth · equal divine concern · soteriological assessment · *An Interpretation of Religion* · *God and the Universe of Faiths***

**How to use them:** state the hypothesis in one sentence and then give the four-step argument as four numbered steps. Examiners reward a reconstructed argument far more than a described position.

#### VISUAL — THE COPERNICAN SHIFT AND THE FOUR-STEP ARGUMENT

```text
     BEFORE (Ptolemaic)                    AFTER (Copernican)
   ┌────────────────────┐              ┌────────────────────────┐
   │   MY TRADITION at  │              │      THE REAL at       │
   │     the centre     │              │       the centre       │
   │        ·           │   ═══════▶   │           ·            │
   │   others orbit as  │              │  traditions orbit as   │
   │   deficient        │              │  culturally conditioned│
   │   approximations   │              │  AUTHENTIC responses   │
   └────────────────────┘              └────────────────────────┘

   THE ARGUMENT, IN FOUR STEPS — write it numbered
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ (1) DIVERSITY IS PERSISTENT and is strongly correlated     │
   │     with birth context.                                    │
   │ (2) MULTIPLE TRADITIONS demonstrably produce moral and     │
   │     spiritual transformation.                              │
   │ (3) EQUAL DIVINE CONCERN resists an arbitrary exclusivism   │
   │     that would make salvation depend on birthplace.        │
   │ ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
   │ (4) THEREFORE one Real may be authentically experienced     │
   │     through different conceptual schemes.                   │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
     Note the shape: (1) is descriptive, (2) is evaluative,
     (3) is theological, (4) is the hypothesis they jointly support.
     Attack any premise and the conclusion is only a possibility claim.
```

*The two circles show the structural move; the numbered box is the argument you must be able to reproduce. Premises 1–3 come from three different domains, which is both the argument's strength and the place where it is most easily broken.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] The doctrine statement.** The major traditions are culturally conditioned human responses to the transcategorial Real, assessed soteriologically by the transformation from self-centredness to Reality-centredness.

**[FACT] Step 1 — persistent diversity.** Religious diversity is persistent and often linked to birth context. The observation is not merely sociological: it is doing argumentative work, because it makes any claim to unique access look like an accident of geography.

**[FACT] Step 2 — transformation occurs plurally.** Multiple traditions produce moral and spiritual transformation. Hick's evidence is the existence of saints, and the moral fruits of practice, across traditions — which is why the criterion he later adopts is soteriological rather than doctrinal.

**[FACT] Step 3 — equal divine concern.** A God of equal concern for all human beings resists an arbitrary exclusivism under which salvation tracks birthplace. **[INFERENCE]** Note that this premise is *theological*, so it is available against a theistic exclusivist but not against a non-theistic one — a discrimination worth one line in a 20-marker.

**[FACT] Step 4 — the conclusion.** Therefore one Real may be authentically experienced through different conceptual schemes. **[INFERENCE]** The conclusion is a **possibility** claim supported by an inference to the best explanation; it is not a demonstration, and Hick does not present it as one.

**[FACT] The Copernican analogy, stated exactly.** Hick shifts the centre from one religion or incarnation to the Real, analogously to a Copernican shift from an ecclesial centre to ultimate Reality. **[INFERENCE]** The analogy is doing rhetorical work as well as structural work: it casts exclusivism as pre-scientific. That is a rhetorical liability an examiner may reward you for noticing.

**[FACT] What the Real is.** The Real in itself is **transcategorial**: personal and non-personal predicates apply to its experienced manifestations, not literally to the Real as it is independently of human schemes. So "God is personal" and "the ultimate is beyond personality" are not, on this account, contradictories about the same object under the same description.

**[FACT] What the hypothesis is *not*.** It is a second-order philosophical hypothesis about the traditions, not a rival first-order creed. Hick's reply to the charge of arrogance depends on this distinction, and Session 8 shows why critics deny that it works.

**Objection.** The argument moves from "many traditions transform people" to "many traditions are authentic responses to the same Real" — but transformation could have a purely natural explanation and would then support no religious conclusion at all.
**Reply.** **[INFERENCE]** Hick's hypothesis is offered as the best explanation *given* that religious experience is taken to be, at least sometimes, cognitive; against a thoroughgoing naturalist it is not intended to be persuasive. Conceding that limitation openly is stronger than pretending the argument is a proof, and it locates the debate correctly — with the Religious Experience owner, where veridicality is decided.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** Hick's pluralistic hypothesis relocates the centre of the religious universe from a tradition to the transcategorial Real, and defends that relocation by an inference to the best explanation rather than by a demonstration.
> **Named evidence:** The argument runs in four steps in *An Interpretation of Religion* — persistent diversity correlated with birth; transformation produced across traditions; equal divine concern telling against an arbitrary exclusivism; and therefore one Real authentically experienced through different conceptual schemes.
> **Analysis:** Because the premises are drawn from three different domains — descriptive, evaluative and theological — the argument is broad but breakable: a non-theist is untouched by premise 3, and a naturalist reads premise 2 differently.
> **Qualification:** The conclusion is a possibility claim about how diversity may be interpreted, offered as second-order philosophy rather than as a new creed, and it should be presented in that modest form because that is the form in which Hick's reply to his critics is available.

#### UPSC TRAPS

13. ⚠️ Do not describe Hick's hypothesis without reconstructing the argument. "Hick says all religions respond to the Real" is a description; the four steps are the answer.
14. ⚠️ Do not say Hick proves pluralism. He offers an inference to the best explanation supporting a possibility claim, and overstating it invites an easy refutation.
15. ⚠️ Do not use the Copernican analogy as though it were itself an argument. It is a picture of the structural move, and an examiner will notice if it is doing work that the premises should be doing.

#### MINI RECAP

Centre moves from tradition to the Real. Four steps: persistent birth-linked diversity; plural transformation; equal divine concern; therefore one Real through many schemes. Transcategorial Real. Second-order hypothesis, not a creed. Possibility claim, not proof.

---

### SESSION 6 — HICK II: THE KANTIAN BASIS, AND THE NOUMENAL OBJECTION

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** Hick borrows Kant's distinction between the world as it is in itself and the world as it appears to us, and applies it to religion: the Real in itself is one thing, and the Gods and Absolutes of the traditions are how that one thing appears through different human conceptual schemes.

**Technical definition.** The **Kantian analogy** distinguishes the Real *in itself* (the Real *an sich*) from humanly experienced religious **phenomena**. Human conceptual schemes — theistic and non-theistic, personal and non-personal — function like categories that shape the appearance. Unlike Kant, however, Hick treats the noumenal Real as **causally related** to religious experience, and that departure is what generates the central objection.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Kantian analogy is what makes Hick's pluralism a philosophical position rather than a pious hope — and it is also what makes it vulnerable, because a Real placed beyond every human category cannot then be described as existing, as causing experience or as good without the categories being smuggled back in.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**Real *an sich* · noumenal / phenomenal · transcategorial · conceptual scheme · categories of religious experience · personal *personae* and non-personal *impersonae* · causal relation to experience · noumenal objection · illicit predication · *nirguṇa* parallel**

**How to use them:** state the analogy, state the *departure* from Kant, and then state the objection that the departure creates. That three-move sequence is the whole session, and it is what a 20-marker rewards.

#### VISUAL — THE ANALOGY, THE DEPARTURE AND THE OBJECTION

```text
  KANT                                HICK
  ───────────────────────────────     ────────────────────────────────
  thing-in-itself (noumenon)          THE REAL AN SICH
        │ unknowable                        │ transcategorial
        │ but causally affects us?          │ CAUSALLY related to
        │ (a famously disputed point)       │ religious experience
        ↓                                   ↓
  CATEGORIES of the understanding     RELIGIOUS CONCEPTUAL SCHEMES
  (space, time, causality)            (theistic / non-theistic;
        ↓                              personal / non-personal)
        ↓                                   ↓
  PHENOMENA — the world as            THE GODS AND ABSOLUTES of the
  experienced                         traditions, as experienced
  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  THE OBJECTION, STATED PRECISELY
  If the Real is beyond ALL categories, then:
     "the Real EXISTS"          ← existence is a category
     "the Real CAUSES experience"← causality is a category
     "the Real is GOOD"          ← evaluative predication
  ...are all illicit. The hypothesis needs the Real to do work that
  its own transcategoriality forbids it from doing.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  THE INDIAN PARALLEL WORTH ONE LINE:
  nirguṇa Brahman (without qualities) is likewise beyond exhaustive
  predication, yet Advaita has a BUILT-IN two-level semantics
  (nirguṇa / saguṇa) that Hick has to construct.
```

*Read the two columns downward to see the analogy, then read the bottom box to see the price. The Indian parallel is not decoration: it is the discriminating point in the owner's parity bench.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] The analogy.** The Kantian analogy distinguishes the Real in itself from humanly experienced phenomena. The traditions' Gods and Absolutes are the Real as experienced through particular conceptual schemes, not the Real as it is independently of those schemes.

**[FACT] The departure from Kant.** Hick treats the noumenal Real as **causally related** to religious experience. That is a stronger commitment than the analogy strictly licenses, and it is deliberate: without it, the Real would explain nothing.

**[FACT] The presupposition being made.** Two things are presupposed: that the Kantian appearance/reality distinction **can be extended religiously**, and that soteriological transformation is **comparable across traditions**. Both are contestable; naming them is worth a mark.

**[FACT] The noumenal objection.** If the Real is unknowable and beyond categories, then even **existence**, **causality** and **goodness** appear to be illicitly predicated of it. The hypothesis needs the Real to be there, to be the source of experience, and to be worth being transformed toward — and each of those is a categorial claim.

**[FACT] Hick's line of reply.** The predicates are said to apply **formally** rather than substantively: the Real is that to which the traditions respond, and formal or purely referential characterisations do not import the substantive content that transcategoriality forbids. **[INFERENCE]** The reply is intelligible but thin, and the honest verdict is that the objection reduces the informativeness of the hypothesis rather than refuting it outright. An answer that says exactly this is at the top band.

**[FACT] The Advanced-dossier form of the same objection.** The dossier records the objection as: *the unknowable Real is too thin to ground determinate religions* — with the reply that Hick presents a second-order hypothesis about transformation, not a new first-order creed, and the note that **revisionism remains a live charge**.

**[FACT] The Indian comparator.** Advaita's **without-qualities (*nirguṇa*)** Brahman is likewise beyond exhaustive predication, and Advaita has a **built-in two-level semantics** — *nirguṇa* and *with-qualities* (*saguṇa*) — where Hick must construct one. **[INFERENCE]** This is the owner's discriminating point: the Indian resource already contains the device Hick needs, which is a genuinely comparative observation rather than a patriotic one.

**Objection.** If the Real is transcategorial, the traditions are not really talking about it, and Hick has replaced a plurality of religions with a single unknowable X that no one worships.
**Reply.** **[FACT]** Hick's answer is that the traditions' *personae* and *impersonae* are authentic — the worship is directed at something genuinely encountered, under a scheme. **[INFERENCE]** The residual worry is not that worship is misdirected but that the theory has purchased compatibility by making the shared object contentless; and the fair verdict is that this is a cost, honestly borne, not a knock-down refutation.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** Hick's pluralism rests on a religious application of the Kantian distinction between reality in itself and reality as humanly experienced, and its central difficulty follows directly from that basis.
> **Named evidence:** The Real *an sich* is transcategorial, so personal and non-personal predicates attach to its manifestations rather than to it; yet Hick, departing from Kant, treats the noumenal Real as causally related to religious experience, and describes it as existing and as that toward which transformation is oriented.
> **Analysis:** If the Real lies beyond all categories, existence, causality and goodness cannot be literally predicated of it, so the hypothesis needs from the Real precisely the work its transcategoriality forbids; Hick's reply is that such predications are formal rather than substantive, which preserves consistency at the cost of informativeness.
> **Qualification:** The objection therefore thins the hypothesis rather than refuting it, and it is worth noting that Advaita's *nirguṇa*–*saguṇa* distinction supplies from within a tradition the two-level semantics Hick has to construct from outside.

#### UPSC TRAPS

16. ⚠️ Do not state the Kantian analogy without stating the departure. Hick's causal claim about the noumenal Real is exactly what the objection targets, and omitting it makes the objection look unmotivated.
17. ⚠️ Do not treat the noumenal objection as a refutation. It reduces informativeness; saying so is more accurate and scores higher than declaring Hick defeated.
18. ⚠️ Do not import the *nirguṇa* comparison as mere decoration. Its examinable point is structural — Advaita has the two-level semantics built in, and Hick must construct it.

#### MINI RECAP

Kant's noumenon/phenomenon applied to religion. Departure: the Real causes experience. Objection: existence, causality and goodness become illicit predications. Reply: formal, not substantive, predication. Verdict: thinner, not refuted. Indian comparator: *nirguṇa*/*saguṇa* built in.

---

### SESSION 7 — HICK III: THE SALVATION-AND-LIBERATION TRANSFORMATION CRITERION

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** If we cannot settle which religion is doctrinally right, Hick proposes we look at what religions *do* to people: the test is whether a tradition actually moves people from being centred on themselves to being centred on ultimate Reality.

**Technical definition.** The **soteriological criterion** assesses traditions by the transformation they effect from **self-centredness to Reality-centredness**. It is a *practical* criterion, offered because doctrinal comparison is deadlocked. Crucially, it does **not** make contradictory doctrines jointly true; it is a criterion of authenticity for traditions, not of truth for propositions.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Hick's criterion answers a question about *traditions* and leaves a question about *propositions* untouched, and the whole force of his position — and the whole force of the objection to it — turns on whether that division of labour is legitimate or merely convenient.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**soteriological criterion · self-centredness to Reality-centredness · transformation · practical test · saints across traditions · fruits · authenticity of a tradition ≠ truth of a proposition · identity and mission objection · self-referential objection · parity objection**

**How to use them:** the sentence that must appear is "**the criterion does not make contradictory doctrines jointly true**". Writing it pre-empts the strongest quick objection and shows the examiner you know what the criterion is for.

#### VISUAL — WHAT THE CRITERION MEASURES, AND WHAT IT LEAVES ALONE

```text
  DOCTRINAL COMPARISON — DEADLOCKED
  "God is personal"  ⟷  "the ultimate is beyond personality"
  "one life, then judgement" ⟷ "beginningless rebirth"
        │  no neutral adjudicating framework available
        ↓
  HICK'S MOVE: change the QUESTION, not the answer
        ↓
  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  SOTERIOLOGICAL CRITERION                                 │
  │  self-centredness  ───────────────▶  Reality-centredness  │
  │  (grasping, ego,                    (compassion, justice, │
  │   fear, acquisition)                 detachment, love)    │
  │  measured by: saints, moral fruits, transformed lives     │
  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        ↓ WHAT IT SETTLES        ↓ WHAT IT DOES NOT SETTLE
   whether a tradition is    which doctrines are TRUE;
   an AUTHENTIC response     whether the ends are the SAME
   ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   THREE OBJECTIONS THAT ARRIVE TOGETHER
   (a) NOUMENAL   — the Real is too thin to be the terminus
                     of transformation (Session 6)
   (b) SELF-REFERENTIAL / PARITY — pluralism criticises first-order
                     superiority while claiming second-order superiority
   (c) IDENTITY / MISSION — traditions object to being redescribed
                     as culturally limited appearances
```

*The top shows why a practical criterion is proposed at all; the box states it; the two arrows show its exact scope; the bottom strip lists the three objections that must be answered together rather than one at a time.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] The criterion.** Transformation from self-centredness to Reality-centredness is the practical criterion. Traditions are assessed by whether they actually produce that reorientation.

**[FACT] Its exact scope.** It **does not** make contradictory doctrines jointly true. That sentence is the owner's, and it is the single most useful line in the session: the criterion establishes the *authenticity of a tradition as a response*, not the *truth of its propositions*.

**[FACT] Why the criterion is proposed.** Because doctrinal comparison across traditions lacks a neutral adjudicating framework — the point Session 16 makes technically as **deep disagreement**. A practical criterion is a way of getting traction where a doctrinal one cannot.

**[FACT] Objection (a) — the noumenal objection returns.** If the Real is transcategorial, what exactly is one becoming centred *on*? Session 6 gives the reply; here the point is that the thinness of the Real infects the criterion as well as the metaphysics.

**[FACT] Objection (b) — the self-referential or parity objection.** Pluralism presents itself as a superior second-order account while criticising first-order claims to superiority. Exclusivists ask why Hick's meta-framework escapes the contestability he attributes to everyone else. **[INFERENCE]** Note that this objection does not require the exclusivist to be right about anything else; it is a consistency challenge, which is why it is so difficult to shake off.

**[FACT] Objection (c) — the identity and mission concern.** Traditions may reasonably object that being redescribed as culturally limited appearances **erases their self-understanding** and weakens witness or mission. The owner's instruction is explicit: *dialogue requires hearing that objection, not declaring it solved.*

**[INFERENCE] How to hold the three together.** They are not three separate complaints but one complaint at three levels — metaphysical (the Real is thin), epistemic (the theory claims a standpoint it denies to others), and existential (the traditions do not recognise themselves in the description). An answer that says this has organised the objection better than the objection is usually organised.

**[FACT] The criticism the owner records about conflicting salvific goals.** Communion with God, non-dual knowledge and liberation-as-cessation (*nirvāṇa*) are not obviously **one** end. Hick's reply appeals to family resemblance or to the transformation criterion; **critics call this flattening**. Session 9 shows Heim taking exactly this criticism and building an alternative pluralism on it.

**Objection.** The criterion is unfalsifiable: any tradition can be said to produce transformation in some of its adherents, so the test excludes nothing.
**Reply.** **[INFERENCE]** It is not vacuous — it excludes traditions or movements whose observable fruit is systematically the intensification of self-assertion — but it is coarse-grained, and it cannot discriminate between two traditions that both produce saints. Conceding the coarseness is better than defending a precision the criterion does not have; and once conceded, the honest conclusion is that the criterion supports pluralism as an **ethic of engagement** rather than as a demonstrated metaphysics.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** Hick assesses traditions soteriologically, by the transformation from self-centredness to Reality-centredness, because doctrinal comparison across traditions has no neutral adjudicating framework.
> **Named evidence:** The criterion is practical and observational — it appeals to saints and moral fruits across traditions — and Hick is explicit that it does not render contradictory doctrines jointly true.
> **Analysis:** Its scope is therefore the authenticity of a tradition as a response rather than the truth of its propositions, which lets it operate where doctrinal comparison is deadlocked; but it inherits the thinness of the Real, it claims a second-order standpoint whose superiority the theory itself criticises in first-order form, and it redescribes traditions in terms they reject.
> **Qualification:** The criterion is also coarse-grained, since it cannot discriminate between traditions that both produce transformed lives, so the defensible conclusion is that it supports pluralism as an ethic of engagement rather than as a demonstrated metaphysics.

#### UPSC TRAPS

19. ⚠️ Do not present the transformation criterion as settling the truth question. It does not, and Hick does not claim that it does.
20. ⚠️ Do not answer the parity objection by repeating that Hick's is a philosophical hypothesis. That reply is available but contested; Session 8 shows D'Costa pressing it, and a script must acknowledge the pressure.
21. ⚠️ Do not omit the identity and mission concern. It is the objection traditions themselves raise, and the owner requires that it be heard rather than declared solved.

#### MINI RECAP

Criterion: self-centredness → Reality-centredness. Scope: authenticity of traditions, not truth of propositions. Three objections at three levels: thin Real, parity, redescription. Conflicting ends: Hick says family resemblance, critics say flattening — and Heim builds on that.

---

### SESSION 8 — EXCLUSIVISM DEFENDED (PLANTINGA), AND PLURALISM ACCUSED (D'COSTA)

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** This session turns the argument round. Plantinga argues that the exclusivist is not doing anything the pluralist is not also doing; D'Costa argues that pluralism is exclusivism wearing a different hat.

**Technical definition.** Plantinga's "A Defense of Religious Exclusivism" answers three charges — **arbitrariness**, **arrogance** and **irrationality** — by showing that each applies equally to the pluralist's own contested second-order belief. D'Costa's charge is that pluralism is a **covert exclusivism**: it makes its own second-order account of the Real uniquely true and reinterprets every tradition against that tradition's self-understanding.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> An answer that presents exclusivism only as intolerance and pluralism only as generosity will not reach the top band, because both positions have serious defenders — and the sharpest exchange in the whole clause is between Plantinga, who shows that the pluralist holds a contested belief too, and D'Costa, who shows that the pluralist's contested belief is doing exactly the work the exclusivist's belief was accused of doing.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**arbitrariness · arrogance · irrationality · warrant · defeater · meta-level parity · contingency of birth · covert exclusivism · second-order account · revisionism · self-understanding of traditions**

**How to use them:** give Plantinga's three replies as three replies, then give the two counters (permissibility ≠ truth; contingency of birth), then give D'Costa in one sentence. That sequence is a complete top-band paragraph.

#### VISUAL — THE THREE CHARGES, THE THREE REPLIES, AND THE COUNTER

```text
  CHARGE                    PLANTINGA'S REPLY               STATUS
  ───────────────────────  ────────────────────────────    ─────────
  1. ARBITRARY:             The PLURALIST also holds a       parity
     you pick your          belief the exclusivist           granted
     tradition without      rejects (that no tradition
     neutral grounds        is uniquely true) — at the
                            meta-level, same position
  ───────────────────────  ────────────────────────────    ─────────
  2. ARROGANT:              Believing p while knowing        parity
     you believe others     others deny p is the             granted
     are wrong              ordinary condition of EVERY
                            philosopher, pluralists
                            included
  ───────────────────────  ────────────────────────────    ─────────
  3. IRRATIONAL:            Awareness of disagreement        partial
     you persist against    may REDUCE warrant; it does      (see
     informed dissent       not EXTINGUISH it                Session 16)
  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  THE TWO COUNTERS THAT MUST FOLLOW
  (i)  The replies establish PERMISSIBILITY, not TRUTH.
  (ii) They do not address CONTINGENCY OF BIRTH: that one's
       tradition is overwhelmingly PREDICTED by birthplace.
  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  D'COSTA, IN ONE MOVE
  Pluralism → makes its OWN second-order account uniquely true
            → reinterprets every tradition against that tradition's
              own self-understanding
            → therefore it IS an exclusivism, about a different object
  Hick's reply: "mine is a philosophical hypothesis, not a rival creed"
  Verdict: the reply does not FULLY escape the charge.
```

*Read the table left to right for the exchange, then the two boxes below for what the exchange does and does not settle. The last box is the strongest single objection to Hick in the entire clause.*

#### TEACHING

**[FACT] Why the session is required.** An answer presenting exclusivism only as intolerance and pluralism only as generosity will not reach the top band. Both positions have serious defenders, and the graded ladder rewards stating Plantinga's defence and then meeting it.

**[FACT] Reply 1 — arbitrariness.** The pluralist too holds a belief — that no tradition is uniquely true — which the exclusivist rejects. At the meta-level the pluralist is therefore in exactly the same position: holding a contested belief without neutral adjudication.

**[FACT] Reply 2 — arrogance.** Believing *p* while knowing that others deny *p* is the ordinary condition of every philosopher, including the pluralist. If that is arrogance here, it is arrogance everywhere; so either the charge is universal and therefore harmless, or it is not a charge at all.

**[FACT] Reply 3 — irrationality.** Awareness of disagreement may reduce warrant somewhat, but it does not extinguish it. Session 16 gives this claim its technical setting in the steadfastness position.

**[FACT] Counter 1.** The replies establish **permissibility**, not **truth**. Showing that exclusivism is rationally permissible is not showing that it is correct — and a script that stops at Plantinga has answered a different question from the one printed.

**[FACT] Counter 2 — contingency of birth.** The replies do not address the fact that one's tradition is **overwhelmingly predicted by where one was born**. This is the objection that actually bites, and it is also premise 1 of Hick's argument, which is why Sessions 5 and 8 lock together.

**[FACT] D'Costa's charge.** Pluralism is a **covert exclusivism**: it makes its own second-order account of the Real the uniquely true one and reinterprets every tradition against its own self-understanding. The owner records this as **the strongest single objection to Hick**, and records that Hick's reply — that his is a philosophical hypothesis, not a rival creed — **does not fully escape it**.

**[INFERENCE] Why the reply does not fully escape.** A second-order theory that tells first-order traditions what they are *really* doing is exercising exactly the authority it denies them. The distinction between orders is real, but it does not by itself confer neutrality; it relocates the claim to privileged access rather than dissolving it.

**Objection.** If Plantinga and D'Costa are both right, the debate is a stalemate and no position is preferable.
**Reply.** **[INFERENCE]** Not a stalemate, but a change of question. What survives is that no position escapes holding a contested commitment — so the live question becomes which commitments are held **corrigibly**, with the procedural virtues that Session 16 shows deep disagreement makes obligatory. That reframing is the conclusion a top-band script draws, and it is defensible without conceding relativism.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH

> **Claim:** The exclusivism–pluralism dispute cannot be settled by attributing bad epistemic character to either side, because each side's defenders show that the other holds an equally contested commitment.
> **Named evidence:** Plantinga's "A Defense of Religious Exclusivism" answers arbitrariness by noting that the pluralist's own denial of unique truth is contested, answers arrogance by observing that believing what others deny is the ordinary condition of philosophy, and answers irrationality by distinguishing reduced from extinguished warrant; D'Costa answers in the other direction, arguing that pluralism makes its own second-order account uniquely true and reinterprets traditions against their self-understanding.
> **Analysis:** Plantinga's replies establish permissibility rather than truth and leave the contingency-of-birth objection untouched, while D'Costa's charge shows that Hick's appeal to a second-order status relocates rather than removes the claim to privileged access.
> **Qualification:** The exchange therefore does not decide between the positions; it converts the question from *who is neutral* — nobody is — into *whose commitment is held corrigibly*, which is the form in which the dispute can still be argued.

#### UPSC TRAPS

22. ⚠️ Do not omit Plantinga's defence from an exclusivism-versus-pluralism answer. The verdict ladder places "Plantinga's defence stated and met" in the strong band; omitting it caps the script lower.
23. ⚠️ Do not state Plantinga's replies without the two counters. Permissibility is not truth, and contingency of birth is untouched — both must appear or the answer looks partisan.
24. ⚠️ Do not treat D'Costa's charge as a minor quibble. The owner records it as the strongest single objection to Hick, and records that Hick's reply does not fully escape it.

#### MINI RECAP

Three charges, three parity replies. Two counters: permissibility ≠ truth; contingency of birth stands. D'Costa: pluralism is covert exclusivism, because it privileges its own second-order account and redescribes traditions. Result: no neutral standpoint; the live question is corrigibility.

---

### SESSION 9 — FOUR VARIETIES OF PLURALISM, AND WHY THEY ARE INCOMPATIBLE

#### DEFINITION

**Plain-language definition.** "Pluralism" names at least four different theories. One says there is one Reality behind many faiths. One says the faiths are heading to genuinely different destinations. One says dialogue should change both sides rather than explain them. One says there are several ultimates.

**Technical definition.** **Identist pluralism** (Hick): one Real, many culturally conditioned responses, assessed soteriologically. **Differential pluralism** (S. Mark Heim, *Salvations*, 1995): traditions aim at and attain **genuinely different religious ends**. **Mutual transformation** (Raimon Panikkar; John Cobb): dialogue changes both parties rather than subsuming them under a neutral hypothesis. **Deep religious pluralism** (David Ray Griffin): a process-based version allowing **plural ultimates** — creativity, God, the cosmos.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The single most common structural error in this clause is to treat "pluralism" as one position and then attack or defend it wholesale; in fact identist and differential pluralism give incompatible answers to the very question the stem is asking, since one says the traditions are aiming at the same thing under different descriptions and the other says they are arriving at different things.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**identist pluralism · differential pluralism · *Salvations* (1995) · plural religious ends · communion · non-dual realisation · liberation-as-cessation (*nirvāṇa*) · mutual transformation · Panikkar · Cobb · deep religious pluralism · Griffin · plural ultimates · fidelity to self-understanding**

**How to use them:** name at least two varieties and state the discriminating question — *one ultimate or several ends?* — because that question is what makes the classification analytical rather than a list.

#### VISUAL — THE DISCRIMINATING QUESTION, AND FOUR ANSWERS

```text
        DISCRIMINATING QUESTION:  ONE ULTIMATE, OR SEVERAL ENDS?
                                   │
        ┌──────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┐
        ↓                          ↓                           ↓
  ONE ULTIMATE              SEVERAL REAL ENDS           SEVERAL ULTIMATES
  ┌──────────────┐          ┌────────────────┐          ┌───────────────┐
  │ HICK         │          │ HEIM           │          │ GRIFFIN       │
  │ identist     │          │ differential   │          │ deep religious│
  │ pluralism    │          │ pluralism      │          │ pluralism     │
  │ one Real,    │          │ Salvations,    │          │ process-based;│
  │ many culturally│        │ 1995           │          │ creativity,   │
  │ conditioned  │          │ communion ≠    │          │ God, cosmos   │
  │ responses    │          │ non-dual       │          │ as plural     │
  │ criterion:   │          │ realisation ≠  │          │ ultimates     │
  │ soteriological│         │ nirvāṇa        │          │               │
  │ transformation│         │ = 3 DESTINATIONS│         │               │
  └──────────────┘          └────────────────┘          └───────────────┘
        │                          │
        │  PANIKKAR / COBB — MUTUAL TRANSFORMATION
        └──────────────▶  dialogue CHANGES BOTH parties rather than
                          subsuming them under a neutral hypothesis
  ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  WHY IT MATTERS FOR MARKS
  Hick is MOST exposed to D'Costa's covert-exclusivism charge.
```
