- **D'Costa:** pluralism is a **covert exclusivism** — its second-order account is held uniquely true and reinterprets traditions against their self-understanding. **Strongest single objection to Hick**; his reply does not fully escape it.
- Net result: no neutral standpoint; the live question is **corrigibility**.

### 9. Four varieties of pluralism

- Discriminating question: **one ultimate, or several ends?**
- **Hick — identist:** one Real, many conditioned responses; criterion soteriological.
- **Heim — differential** (*Salvations*, 1995): communion, non-dual realisation, cessation are **three destinations**; criterion fidelity to self-understanding; cost — **gives up a single ultimate**.
- **Panikkar / Cobb — mutual transformation:** dialogue changes both parties; a method, not a metaphysics.
- **Griffin — deep religious pluralism:** process-based, **plural ultimates** (creativity, God, cosmos).
- Verdict: the triad is a **map, not a verdict**; Hick most exposed to D'Costa, Heim most faithful to the traditions.

### 10. The Vedic verse, and its discipline

- *Ekaṃ sad viprā bahudhā vadanti* — **Ṛgveda 1.164.46**, *asya vāmasya* riddle-hymn, ascribed to **Dīrghatamas**.
- Native subject: **naming of Vedic deities** — Indra, Mitra, Varuṇa, Agni, Garutmān, Yama, Mātariśvan.
- Four disciplines: cite precisely · state the native subject · **argue** the modern extension · keep transliteration consistent.
- Structure supplied: **one referent, many namings** — which is what breaks the absolute-truth inference.
- Weakest point of the extension: within the pantheon the referent is agreed; across traditions it is contested.

### 11. Vivekananda's Universal Religion (2025 owner)

- Argument: capacities vary → disciplines vary → non-dual ground → **realisation**, not doctrinal uniformity, is the test.
- Four disciplines matched to dispositions: knowledge (*jñāna*), devotion (*bhakti*), action (*karma*), meditative discipline (*rāja*).
- **Chicago, 1893:** acceptance, **not mere toleration** — toleration still condescends.
- Harmony **without homogenisation**; "all religions say the same thing" is **not safe**.
- Presupposition: Vedānta as a **non-sectarian meta-framework**.
- Objection: **neo-Vedāntic assimilation**. Reply: distinctive forms preserved. Status: **contested** — say so.
- Advaita ground has a limit: it does not follow without argument that communion, creator-relation and cessation are one end.

### 12. Radhakrishnan and Gandhi

- **Radhakrishnan:** religions are diverse **interpretations of a common spiritual experience** (*anubhava*); criterion **integral experience**; risk **perennialist reduction**; *The Hindu View of Life*.
- **Gandhi:** **equal respect + fallibilism**; summarised by *sarva-dharma-samabhāva* — a **modern summary formula**, not an unverified verbatim slogan.
- Division of labour: Radhakrishnan **explains** difference (needs a contested uniformity claim); Gandhi **prescribes** how to live with it (needs only a claim about one's own limits).
- Gandhi's fallibilism is therefore the more **portable** resource — available to exclusivist, inclusivist and pluralist alike.

### 13. Many-sidedness as a logic

- **Anekāntavāda** (reality is many-sided) → **nayavāda** (name the standpoint) → **syādvāda** (*syāt* = "in a certain respect") → **saptabhaṅgī** (sevenfold predication).
- Not relativism: the respects are **stated and checkable**; predication is regulated, not abandoned.
- Two live uses: denies that a finite formulation exhausts the absolute; marks framework-relativity **inside** the proposition (deep disagreement).
- Owner's note: stating it as a **logic** is worth more than describing the elephant parable.
- Limit: same question, same respect ⇒ no index of standpoint helps.

### 14. Absolute truth without epistemic absolutism

- Suppressed premise of the 2022 inference: **a finite formulation can exhaust the absolute**.
- Three independent denials: Advaita's *nirguṇa* (with *saguṇa* structuring devotion) · Jain *syādvāda* · Hick's transcategorial Real.
- **Absolute referent + pluralist access.**
- Four separations: **referent · formulation · access · salvation**.
- Three independent theses: **ontological absolutism · epistemic fallibilism · political tolerance**.
- Presupposition made: truth admits **degrees of adequacy or non-literal mediation**.
- Template: "Ontological absolutism entails epistemic exclusivism only if one adds the premise that a finite formulation can exhaust the absolute."

### 15. Hard contradiction, and the 2023 charges

- Four legitimate reinterpretive moves: **level · symbol · standpoint · soteriological end**.
- Residue: a personal creator cannot both exist and not exist in the same respect; the self cannot be both permanent and denied in the same sense. **Acknowledge, do not harmonise.**
- 2023 contains **two** allegations: (a) invites conflict; (b) destroys the truth of religion.
- Reply (a): conflict is not caused by plurality alone — power, identity and exclusivist certainty mediate it.
- Reply (b): pluralism **relocates** absoluteness in the transcendent Real; coexistence ≠ "anything goes".
- Alethic exclusivity on one proposition is compatible with soteriological and political pluralism.

### 16. Deep and peer disagreement

- **Peer** = roughly equal in evidence and reasoning ability.
- **Conciliationism / equal weight:** Feldman, Christensen — reduce confidence. **Steadfastness:** Kelly, van Inwagen — one's own reasoning is part of one's evidence.
- **Independence principle** — conciliationism needs it; steadfastness denies it.
- **Fogelin (1985):** deep disagreement = no shared **framework propositions**; Wittgenstein's **hinges**, *On Certainty*. **Lynch:** extends to epistemic principles, where defence is circular.
- Religion differs over **sources · criteria · framework**.
- Four consequences: failure of convergence is predicted (weak evidence for relativism) · exclusivism not convicted of irrationality · pluralism not established by persistence · **procedural virtues** obligatory.
- Payoff: **unresolvability is epistemic, not alethic.**

### 17. Toleration

- Three components: **objection · acceptance · power**. Remove (i) ⇒ indifference; remove (iii) ⇒ endurance.
- Paradox: allowing what one holds wrong. Resolution **by levels** — first-order disapproval, second-order reasons against coercion.
- **Forst's ladder:** permission · coexistence · respect · esteem. Only respect and esteem fit **equal citizenship**; most historical tolerance was **permission**.
- **Locke 1689:** no commission over souls · force cannot produce belief · coerced conformity would not save.
- **Mill 1859:** harm principle + the epistemic cost of suppression. **Popper:** paradox of tolerance, restricted to those who answer argument with violence. **Rawls:** overlapping consensus.
- **Aśoka RE XII:** do not disparage other sects — you injure your own most; **concord (*samavāya*)**; earliest verifiable state-level argument. **Akbar:** *ṣulḥ-i kull* — a **policy**. **Gandhi**, **Vivekananda** (acceptance), **Jain** epistemic ground. Articles 25–28.
- Verdict: **indispensable but insufficient.**

### 18. Religious freedom

- Bundle: conscience (inviolable) · profess · practise · propagate · change or leave · freedom from compelled observance · associational right.
- Four justifications: **incoercibility · autonomy · epistemic · social**.
- Five hard problems: **practice/belief asymmetry** (public order, morality, health — contested terms) · **propagation and conversion** (the line is **coercion and fraud**) · **internal minorities** — Shachar's **paradox of multicultural vulnerability**, the most examinable tension · **neutrality** — wall of separation against **Bhargava's principled distance**, which permits reform · **judicial determination** — "essential religious practices" makes the state an arbiter of theology.
- Objection: a liberal value universalised. Reply: incoercibility is about the **psychology of belief**, and Aśoka, Sufi and Bhakti traditions reach comparable conclusions on their own grounds.
- Verdict: possible and more nearly necessary; never unlimited or perfectly neutral.

### 19. History of religions and conflict

- Three versions: **involvement** (trivial) · **significant cause** (the argument) · **defining engine** (false).
- For: absolute truth-claims + exclusivist soteriology + strong identity + sacralised authority. **Kimball's markers** — a typology, not a law.
- Against: **Cavanaugh** (2009) religious/secular as modern construct · **Armstrong** (2014) no separable pre-modern sphere · **Sen** (2006) singular-affiliation fallacy.
- The move: **cause · marker · mobiliser · legitimiser**; most cases are the last three.
- Counter-evidence: Aśoka after Kaliṅga · monastic learning · hospitals and famine relief · abolitionism · civil rights · *satyāgraha* · Bhakti and Sufi universalism · interfaith movement.
- Verdict: **historically selective and conceptually confused**, with the narrower truth conceded.

### 20. Religion as a uniting force

- For: **Durkheim** (collective effervescence; church as moral community) · universalist ethics (*vasudhaiva kuṭumbakam*, the four abodes, *ummah*, image of God) · **1893 and 1993 Parliaments**, *Declaration Toward a Global Ethic*, **Hans Küng** · transnational relief and diaspora networks.
- Against: Durkheim's integration is **in-group** · **Huntington** — cite as a **thesis under criticism** · **Roy** — deterritorialised, deculturated religiosity produces cosmopolitan **and** reactive forms · instrumentalisation.
- Three conditions: **universalist ethics · fallibilist epistemology · non-monopolised identity**.
- Verdict: **high-amplitude**, not intrinsically unifying — the same traditions produce the Parliament of Religions and communal violence.

### 21. The bench, the grid, the decoder, the ladder, the spine

- **Parity bench (six problems):** rival truth-claims · framework-relativity · grounds of toleration · tolerance versus acceptance · ultimate reality versus its descriptions · salvific ends. Discriminating points: the Vedic verse is about names of deities; Jainism marks relativity inside the proposition; Aśoka is the earliest verifiable state-level argument; Advaita's two-level semantics is built in; Heim fits Indian plurality better than Hick.
- **Inter-thinker grid (nine rows):** exclusivism · inclusivism · Hick · Vivekananda · Radhakrishnan · Jain · Heim · Plantinga · D'Costa, on one ultimate / status of traditions / criterion / main objection.
- **Directive decoder (eleven rows)** — fix the axis; keep the context clause; conditional verdict with conditions; name the central problem in one sentence; grade a sweeping claim; define before arguing importance; show the compatibility mechanism; face the hard cases; test the inference; separate two allegations; be faithful to the named thinker.
- **Verdict ladder:** weak · average · good · strong · top — the top band requires a discriminating distinction **and** an acknowledgement of hard contradictions.
- **Architecture:** 10 marks — define, one model, referent versus access, one objection, non-relativist verdict. 15 marks — E-I-P, thinker's argument and presupposition, comparator, objection and reply. 20 marks — frame, strongest model, referent versus formulation, hard contradictions, two objections and replies, graded conclusion.
- **Corpus signal:** **12 owned parts of 112**; two in each of 2018–2021, one in each of 2022–2025.
- **Safe formulations:** pluralism denies exhaustive possession, not absolute reality · hard contradiction must be routed, not harmonised · toleration from the strong is permission, not equality · religion is a high-amplitude force · unresolvability is epistemic, not alethic.

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 6/31: Hick I - the Copernican revolution and its four-step argument

```ascii-master
    BEFORE (Ptolemaic)                         AFTER (Copernican)
  +----------------------+                +-----------------------------+
  | MY TRADITION at the  |                | THE REAL at the centre      |
  | centre; others orbit |  ==========>   | traditions orbit as         |
  | as DEFICIENT         |                | culturally conditioned      |
  | approximations       |                | AUTHENTIC responses         |
  +----------------------+                +-----------------------------+
THE ARGUMENT - WRITE IT NUMBERED
 (1) DIVERSITY IS PERSISTENT and strongly correlated with BIRTH CONTEXT   [descriptive]
 (2) MULTIPLE TRADITIONS demonstrably produce moral and spiritual transformation [evaluative]
 (3) EQUAL DIVINE CONCERN resists an arbitrary exclusivism under which salvation
     would track birthplace                                              [theological]
 ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 (4) THEREFORE one Real may be AUTHENTICALLY EXPERIENCED through different
     conceptual schemes                                                  [the hypothesis]
NOTE THE SHAPE: three premises from three DIFFERENT domains. That is the argument's breadth
  and its breaking point - premise (3) is unavailable against a NON-THEISTIC exclusivist,
  and a naturalist reads premise (2) differently.
STATUS OF THE CONCLUSION: a POSSIBILITY CLAIM supported by inference to the best explanation.
  Hick does not present it as a demonstration, and overstating it invites easy refutation.
THE REAL IS TRANSCATEGORIAL: personal and non-personal predicates apply to its EXPERIENCED
  MANIFESTATIONS, not literally to the Real as it is independently of human schemes.
  ==> "God is personal" and "the ultimate is beyond personality" are not contradictories
      about the same object under the same description.
WHAT THE HYPOTHESIS IS NOT: a rival first-order creed. It is SECOND-ORDER philosophy - and
  that status is exactly what Hick's reply to the arrogance charge depends on (Panel 10).
SOURCES: An Interpretation of Religion; God and the Universe of Faiths. "Transcategorial"
  is Hick's own later term.
TRAP: describing the position without RECONSTRUCTING the argument caps the script at average.
```

```ascii-master
  KANT                                    HICK
  ------------------------------------    ------------------------------------------

     | unknowable                            | transcategorial

THE DEPARTURE: Hick treats the noumenal Real as CAUSALLY related to religious experience.
  That is stronger than the analogy licenses, and it is DELIBERATE - without it the Real
  would explain nothing.                                     <== NAME THE DEPARTURE
PRESUPPOSITIONS: (i) the Kantian appearance/reality distinction can be extended religiously;
  (ii) soteriological transformation is COMPARABLE across traditions.
THE NOUMENAL OBJECTION, STATED PRECISELY
  If the Real is beyond ALL categories, then:
      "the Real EXISTS"            <- existence is a category
      "the Real CAUSES experience" <- causality is a category
      "the Real is GOOD"           <- evaluative predication
  ...are all ILLICIT. The hypothesis needs the Real to do work its own transcategoriality
  forbids it from doing.
HICK'S LINE OF REPLY: the predicates apply FORMALLY, not substantively - the Real is that to
  which the traditions respond. Consistency preserved; INFORMATIVENESS reduced.
HONEST VERDICT: the objection THINS the hypothesis rather than refuting it. Say exactly that.
ADVANCED-DOSSIER FORM: "the unknowable Real is too thin to ground determinate religions" ->
  reply: a second-order hypothesis about transformation, not a new creed -> note:
  REVISIONISM REMAINS A LIVE CHARGE.
INDIAN COMPARATOR (structural, not decorative): nirguna Brahman (without qualities) is
  likewise beyond exhaustive predication - but Advaita has a BUILT-IN two-level semantics
  (nirguna / saguna) that Hick must CONSTRUCT.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/31: Hick III - the salvation-and-liberation criterion, and its price

```ascii-master
WHY A PRACTICAL CRITERION AT ALL: doctrinal comparison across traditions is DEADLOCKED -
  "God is personal" vs "the ultimate is beyond personality"; "one life then judgement" vs
  "beginningless rebirth" - and there is no neutral adjudicating framework (Panel 20).
THE CRITERION
  +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  SELF-CENTREDNESS  ------------------------->  REALITY-CENTREDNESS       |
  |  (grasping, ego, fear, acquisition)           (compassion, justice,      |
  |                                                detachment, love)         |
  |  measured by: saints, moral fruits, transformed lives across traditions  |
  +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
WHAT IT SETTLES:      whether a TRADITION is an authentic response
WHAT IT DOES NOT:     which DOCTRINES are true; whether the ENDS are the same
  ==> "IT DOES NOT MAKE CONTRADICTORY DOCTRINES JOINTLY TRUE."  <== WRITE THIS SENTENCE
THREE OBJECTIONS THAT ARRIVE TOGETHER - one complaint at three levels
  (a) METAPHYSICAL   the Real is too thin to be the terminus of transformation (Panel 7)
  (b) EPISTEMIC      SELF-REFERENTIAL / PARITY - pluralism criticises first-order claims to
                     superiority while asserting second-order superiority. Exclusivists ask
                     why Hick's meta-framework escapes the contestability he attributes to all.
  (c) EXISTENTIAL    IDENTITY AND MISSION - traditions object that being redescribed as
                     culturally limited appearances ERASES their self-understanding and
                     weakens witness or mission.
                     ==> DIALOGUE REQUIRES HEARING THAT OBJECTION, NOT DECLARING IT SOLVED.
FURTHER OBJECTION: the criterion is COARSE-GRAINED - it excludes movements whose observable
  fruit is systematic self-assertion, but cannot discriminate between two traditions that
  both produce saints.
CONSEQUENCE OF CONCEDING THE COARSENESS: the criterion supports pluralism as an
  ETHIC OF ENGAGEMENT rather than as a DEMONSTRATED METAPHYSICS.
CONFLICTING ENDS: communion / non-dual knowledge / cessation are not obviously ONE end.
  Hick: family resemblance or transformation. Critics: FLATTENING. Heim builds on this (Panel 11).
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 10/31: Pluralism accused - D'Costa's covert-exclusivism charge

```ascii-master
THE CHARGE, IN ONE MOVE
  PLURALISM -> makes its OWN second-order account UNIQUELY TRUE
            -> REINTERPRETS every tradition AGAINST that tradition's self-understanding
            -> therefore it IS an exclusivism, about a different object
STATUS: the OWNER RECORDS THIS AS THE STRONGEST SINGLE OBJECTION TO HICK.
HICK'S REPLY: "mine is a philosophical HYPOTHESIS, not a rival CREED."
OWNER'S VERDICT ON THE REPLY: it DOES NOT FULLY ESCAPE the charge.
WHY IT DOES NOT ESCAPE: a second-order theory that tells first-order traditions what they are
  REALLY doing exercises exactly the adjudicating authority it denies to each of them.
  The distinction between orders is real, but it RELOCATES the claim to privileged access
  rather than dissolving it.
CONVERGENCE: the charge converges with the IDENTITY AND MISSION concern (Panel 8c) that the
  traditions themselves raise - the same complaint, once from philosophy, once from the pews.
IF PLANTINGA AND D'COSTA ARE BOTH RIGHT, IS IT A STALEMATE?
  No - it is a CHANGE OF QUESTION. What survives is that NO position escapes holding a
  contested commitment. The live question becomes WHICH COMMITMENTS ARE HELD CORRIGIBLY,
  with the procedural virtues that deep disagreement makes obligatory (Panel 20).
D'COSTA'S OWN LIMIT: the charge is DIAGNOSTIC, not CONSTRUCTIVE - it shows Hick has not
  achieved neutrality; it does not show exclusivism is TRUE.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/31: The Vedic verse, and the four disciplines that make it usable

```ascii-master
TEXT: "ekam sad vipra bahudha vadanti" - RGVEDA 1.164.46, asya vamasya riddle-hymn,
  ascribed to DIRGHATAMAS. "The one existent, the wise speak of in many ways."
 STEP 1 CITE PRECISELY        RIGHT: "Rgveda 1.164.46"      WRONG: "the Vedas say..."
 STEP 2 STATE THE NATIVE SUBJECT
        the verse names ONE EXISTENT under MANY DIVINE NAMES:
        Indra | Mitra | Varuna | Agni | the celestial Garutman | Yama | Matarisvan
        ==> its own topic is the UNITY OF THE VEDIC PANTHEON,
            NOT the unity of the world's religions
 STEP 3 SUPPLY THE BRIDGING ARGUMENT            <== THIS IS WHERE THE MARKS ARE
        the verse establishes a STRUCTURE: ONE REFERENT / MANY NAMINGS
        the pluralist claim needs that structure applied ACROSS traditions
        the extension is a 19th-20th century development (Vivekananda, Radhakrishnan, Gandhi)
        therefore: ARGUE that the structure generalises - do not assume the ancient text
        already made the modern claim
 STEP 4 KEEP TRANSLITERATION CONSISTENT
        "ekam sad vipra bahudha vadanti" or "ekam sat vipra bahudha vadanti" - both
        acceptable; pick ONE and hold it through the script
WHERE THE EXTENSION IS WEAKEST: inside the pantheon the REFERENT IS AGREED and the dispute is
  nominal. ACROSS traditions the referent itself is in dispute - a personal creator vs a
  non-personal absolute vs no ultimate substance at all. The analogy transfers the FORM of
  one-referent-many-names but NOT the agreement that made the form uncontroversial.
WHY THE DISCIPLINE PAYS: it is precisely BECAUSE the verse separates referent from formulation
  that it supports the absolute-truth answer of Panel 16 - an absolute REFERENT with plural,
  non-exhaustive FORMULATIONS.
DOUBLE FUNCTION: philosophically it supplies the referent/formulation distinction in its
  clearest ancient form; historically it shows the distinction is not a modern liberal import.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/31: Radhakrishnan and Gandhi - explanation and prescription

```ascii-master
  RADHAKRISHNAN - an EXPLANATION of difference
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  common SPIRITUAL EXPERIENCE (anubhava)                               |
  |        |--> interpretation A --> doctrine set A                       |
  |        |--> interpretation B --> doctrine set B                       |
  |        |--> interpretation C --> doctrine set C                       |
  |  CRITERION: INTEGRAL EXPERIENCE                                       |
  |  RISK:      PERENNIALIST REDUCTION - traditions reduced to variant    |
  |             readings of one experience                                |
  |  SOURCE:    The Hindu View of Life                                    |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  GANDHI - a PRESCRIPTION for living with difference
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  EQUAL RESPECT for all religions                                      |
  |      +                                                                |
  |  FALLIBILISM about one's OWN grasp of truth                           |
  |      ==> summarised by the MODERN formula sarva-dharma-samabhava      |
  |  DISCIPLINE: use as a SUMMARY of fallibilist equal respect, NOT as an |
  |              unverified verbatim slogan                               |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
WHAT "PERENNIALIST REDUCTION" MEANS AS AN OBJECTION: if doctrine is interpretation laid over a
  UNIFORM experience, doctrinal disagreement is demoted to variation in commentary. Traditions
  that regard their doctrines as REVEALED will not accept the demotion - the identity
  objection of Panel 8 arriving in Indian dress.
THE DIVISION OF LABOUR (worth one line in the exam)
  Radhakrishnan needs a CONTESTED claim - that religious experience is relevantly UNIFORM
  Gandhi needs only a claim about MY OWN LIMITS
  ==> Gandhi's position survives even if Radhakrishnan's is refuted, which is why his
      fallibilism is the PORTABLE resource - available to exclusivist, inclusivist and
      pluralist alike, and identical with the "procedural virtue" of Panel 20.
OBJECTION TO EQUAL RESPECT: respect that costs nothing is not respect; and one cannot equally
  respect a religion whose central claims one believes false.
REPLY: what is equally respected is not each doctrine's TRUTH but each tradition's STANDING as
  a serious human response to ultimate concern, held by people whose access is no more and no
  less partial than one's own. Equal DIGNITY, not identical PROPOSITIONAL TRUTH (Panel 5).
GANDHI IN THE TOLERATION BENCH: listed with Asoka RE XII and Akbar's sulh-i kull as the Indian
  grounds; Asoka's is the EARLIEST VERIFIABLE STATE-LEVEL argument (Panel 23).
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 15/31: Jain many-sidedness as a logic of standpoints

```ascii-master
THE THREE-LEVEL APPARATUS
  ANEKANTAVADA   (metaphysical)   reality is MANY-SIDED
       v
  NAYAVADA       (methodological) every judgement is made FROM a standpoint; NAME it
       v
  SYADVADA       (logical)        prefix the assertion with "syat" = "in a certain respect"
       v
  SAPTABHANGI    (formal)         the SEVENFOLD scheme of predication
WHY THIS IS NOT "ANYTHING GOES"
  RELATIVISM: "P is true for you, not-P for me, and there is no fact of the matter."
  SYADVADA:   "P holds in respect R1; not-P holds in respect R2. Both are DETERMINATE claims
               about ONE reality; the respects are STATED and can be CHECKED."
  ==> predication is REGULATED, not abandoned.
PRESUPPOSITION: contradictory-seeming claims may concern different ASPECTS, LEVELS or
  PRACTICAL ORIENTATIONS.
CANONICAL EXAMPLE, CORRECTLY WEIGHTED: the blind persons and the elephant illustrate partial
  access - but THE DOCTRINE IS MORE RIGOROUS THAN THE PARABLE. The parable illustrates;
  the sevenfold scheme REGULATES.
TWO LIVE USES
  (a) ABSOLUTE TRUTH: reality is many-sided, every viewpoint is PARTIALLY true, and no partial
      viewpoint may claim exhaustive absoluteness ==> epistemic humility DISSOLVES dogmatic
      exclusivism while leaving an absolute REFERENT intact (Panel 16).
  (b) DEEP DISAGREEMENT: it makes the standpoint (naya) EXPLICIT and prefixes every assertion
      with syat, so framework-relativity is marked INSIDE the proposition rather than left
      implicit ==> A GENUINE INDIAN CONTRIBUTION TO A LIVE WESTERN PROBLEM, and the owner
      records that saying so is worth MORE than describing the elephant parable.
ADVANCED OBJECTION: if every claim is only conditional, no claim has full authority.
  REPLY: Jain logic REGULATES predication by standpoint and object-aspect; it disciplines
  absolutism rather than abolishing truth.
THE LIMIT: some claims contradict on the SAME question in the SAME respect. There, no index of
  standpoint helps - pluralism must distinguish LITERAL CONFLICT, SYMBOLIC LANGUAGE and
  DIFFERENT SOTERIOLOGICAL AIMS, and acknowledge whatever survives (Panel 17).
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 16/31: Absolute truth without epistemic absolutism

```ascii-master
THE ALLEGED INFERENCE (2022 stem, reconstructed)
  P1  There is exactly ONE Absolute Truth.
  P2  [SUPPRESSED]  A finite formulation CAN EXHAUST it.
  P3  [SUPPRESSED]  MY tradition's formulation is that one.
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------
  C   Therefore every other tradition is simply mistaken  ==> EXCLUSIVISM
  THE ARGUMENT IS VALID. It is UNSOUND if P2 or P3 fails.
CONCEDE FIRST: if "absolute truth" MEANS "my religion's propositions are the sole truth",
  the inference succeeds - trivially, because exclusivism was built into the premise.
DENY P2 - THREE INDEPENDENT TRADITIONS ALREADY DO
  ADVAITA  nirguna Brahman (WITHOUT QUALITIES) is beyond exhaustive predication, while
           saguna forms (WITH QUALITIES) structure real devotional paths
  JAINISM  syadvada indexes every assertion to a standpoint (Panel 15)
  HICK     the Real an sich is transcategorial (Panel 7)
  ==> ABSOLUTE REFERENT  +  PLURALIST ACCESS. The link is BROKEN.
FOUR THINGS AN ABSOLUTE-TRUTH ANSWER MUST SEPARATE
  1 REFERENT     what the truth is ABOUT            (one)
  2 FORMULATION  how it is STATED                   (many, non-exhaustive)
  3 ACCESS       who can KNOW it, and how well      (partial, corrigible)
  4 SALVATION    who is LIBERATED by it             (a FURTHER question)
THREE THESES THAT DO NOT ENTAIL ONE ANOTHER
  ONTOLOGICAL ABSOLUTISM  |  EPISTEMIC FALLIBILISM  |  POLITICAL TOLERANCE
  ==> an exclusivist may consistently defend religious freedom (Locke); and tolerance
      cannot be inferred from metaphysics without an ADDITIONAL premise.
PRESUPPOSITION THE REPLY ITSELF MAKES: truth permits DEGREES OF ADEQUACY or NON-LITERAL
  MEDIATION without collapsing into relativism. State it - a critic who denies it reinstates
  the inference, and that is where the argument properly continues.
SELF-REFERENCE WORRY: "there is one absolute Truth" is itself a finite formulation.
  REPLY: the claim is FORMAL (a single referent), not substantive - the same move as Hick's,
  with the same cost (thinness). What it does establish is that the position must be held
  CORRIGIBLY, which is what epistemic fallibilism asserts.
TEMPLATE: "Ontological absolutism entails epistemic exclusivism ONLY IF one adds the premise
  that a finite formulation can exhaust the absolute. Deny that premise - as Advaita's nirguna
  and Jain anekantavada both do - and the inference from absolute truth to exclusivism fails."
2024 STEM ("notion of absolute truth"): a NOTION question - the four-way separation IS the
  analysis, and the compatibility result is the note's conclusion.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/31: What pluralism cannot dissolve - hard contradiction

```ascii-master
APPARENT CONFLICT BETWEEN TRADITIONS - four legitimate reinterpretive moves, and the residue
  +-----------+-----------+------------+----------------+------------------------------+
  | LEVEL     | SYMBOL    | STANDPOINT | SOTERIOLOGICAL | RESIDUE                      |
  |           |           |            | END            |                              |
  | nirguna / | non-      | syat-      | different REAL | SAME question, SAME respect  |
  | saguna;   | literal   | indexed    | ends (Heim):   | ---------------------------- |
  | ultimate /| predi-    | assertion  | communion =/=  | "a personal creator exists"  |
  | conven-   | cation;   | (Jain)     | non-dual =/=   | vs "no such being exists"    |
  | tional    | symbolic  |            | nirvana        | "the self is permanent"      |
  | truth     | reference |            |                | vs "there is no such self"   |
  |-----------+-----------+------------+----------------|------------------------------|
  |     these dissolve APPARENT conflict                | NOT DISSOLVABLE -            |
  |                                                     | ACKNOWLEDGE IT               |
  +-----------------------------------------------------+------------------------------+
THE CONTROL, VERBATIM IN SUBSTANCE: a personal creator cannot both exist and not exist in the
  same respect; the self cannot both be permanent and denied in the same sense. Pluralism may
  reinterpret level, symbol, standpoint or end, BUT IT MUST ACKNOWLEDGE ANY CONTRADICTION
  THAT REMAINS.
WHY CONCEDING THE RESIDUE STRENGTHENS THE ANSWER
  a pluralism that concedes irreducible conflict at some points but maintains authentic plural
  response at others is a DETERMINATE position that can be argued for; a pluralism that
  harmonises everything is UNFALSIFIABLE and, by D'Costa's argument, is quietly imposing its
  own framework on traditions that assert contradictories DELIBERATELY.
OBJECTION: if contradictions remain, at most one tradition is right about them, so pluralism
  has collapsed into exclusivism about those claims.
REPLY: yes - ABOUT THOSE CLAIMS, and that is the correct result, not a defeat. Pluralism is a
  thesis about traditions as wholes and about the availability of truth and transformation
  beyond one's own; it is NOT the thesis that no religious proposition is false. Alethic
  exclusivity on a specific proposition is compatible with soteriological and political
  pluralism - which is exactly what the four axes of Panel 1 were designed to permit.
VERDICT-LADDER NOTE: "explicit acknowledgement of hard contradictions that pluralism cannot
  dissolve" is a TOP-BAND requirement.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 19/31: Peer disagreement - conciliationism against steadfastness

```ascii-master
DEFINITION: an EPISTEMIC PEER is one roughly equal in EVIDENCE and REASONING ABILITY.
  +----------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | CONCILIATIONISM / EQUAL WEIGHT         | STEADFASTNESS                                |
  | Richard Feldman - David Christensen    | Thomas Kelly - Peter van Inwagen             |
  |----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|
  | on discovering that a peer disagrees,  | one MAY retain one's belief, since one's OWN |
  | REDUCE confidence, often toward        | reasoning is part of one's evidence          |
  | suspension                             |                                              |
  |----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|
  | NEEDS the INDEPENDENCE PRINCIPLE:      | DENIES the independence principle            |
  | assess the disagreement WITHOUT relying|                                              |
  | on the reasoning under dispute         |                                              |
  +----------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
VAN INWAGEN'S WELL-KNOWN CLAIM: it is not irrational to retain one's belief even when one
  cannot show that the other party is wrong.
WHY NAMING THE INDEPENDENCE PRINCIPLE MATTERS: it shows WHY the two camps disagree rather than
  merely THAT they do - the whole dispute turns on whether the disputed reasoning may be used
  in assessing the dispute.
CONNECTION TO PANEL 9: Plantinga's third reply - awareness of disagreement REDUCES but does
  not EXTINGUISH warrant - is a steadfast position, and gets its technical backing here.
DISTINCTION TO PROTECT: PEER disagreement assumes a SHARED FRAMEWORK and asks what to do about
  a peer. DEEP disagreement (Panel 20) DENIES the shared framework. Conflating them makes both
  positions unstatable.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/31: Deep disagreement - Fogelin, hinges, and four consequences

```ascii-master
WHY THE MODULE IS NEEDED: the pluralism debate ASSUMES rival truth-claims could in principle be
  settled by argument. The epistemology of disagreement asks whether that assumption is true -
  and its answer RESHAPES THE WHOLE CLAUSE.
FOGELIN 1985, "The Logic of Deep Disagreement"
  some disagreements are NOT RESOLVABLE BY ARGUMENT IN PRINCIPLE, because the parties do not
  share the FRAMEWORK PROPOSITIONS - Wittgenstein's HINGE commitments from ON CERTAINTY -
  against which evidence would count.
  ==> when backgrounds diverge, THE SAME EVIDENCE IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR THE SAME THING,
      and argument has NO SHARED FULCRUM.
MICHAEL LYNCH extends this to disagreement about EPISTEMIC PRINCIPLES themselves - which
  sources are reliable - where any defence is CIRCULAR.
APPLICATION TO RELIGION - the parties differ over
  * SOURCES of evidence      which scripture, which experience, which inference
  * CRITERIA of adequacy     salvation? liberation? cessation?
  * the FRAMEWORK            within which any datum would count at all
FOUR CONSEQUENCES - WRITE THEM AS FOUR, AND NOTE THAT THEY CUT BOTH WAYS
  1 the failure of rational convergence is PREDICTED, not scandalous
    ==> it is WEAK EVIDENCE FOR RELATIVISM
  2 exclusivism CANNOT BE CONVICTED OF IRRATIONALITY merely for persisting
  3 pluralism CANNOT BE ESTABLISHED merely by pointing at persistence
  4 what CAN be rationally required are PROCEDURAL VIRTUES - fallibilism, intellectual
    humility, epistemic charity, and willingness to MAKE ONE'S FRAMEWORK EXPLICIT
THE INDIAN RESOURCE: anekantavada / nayavada / syadvada is in effect a FORMAL APPARATUS for
  exactly this - it makes the standpoint (naya) explicit and prefixes every assertion with
  syat, so framework-relativity is marked INSIDE the proposition. Saying it that way is worth
  more than describing the elephant parable.
OBJECTION: if deep disagreement is unresolvable, pluralism collapses into relativism.
REPLY: UNRESOLVABILITY IS EPISTEMIC, NOT ALETHIC - one party may still be RIGHT; what fails is
  our ability to DEMONSTRATE which.                          <== THE PAYOFF SENTENCE
SECOND OBJECTION: this licenses complacency.
REPLY: it licenses only the RETENTION of belief, not the refusal of dialogue - and it makes the
  procedural virtues OBLIGATORY rather than optional.
VERDICT FORMULA: "Religious disagreement is deep in Fogelin's technical sense: it lacks a
  shared framework against which evidence could adjudicate. That EXPLAINS the persistence of
  exclusivism WITHOUT VINDICATING it, and supports pluralism as an ETHIC OF ENGAGEMENT rather
  than as a demonstrated metaphysics."
PROMOTION NOTE: the Advanced dossier's ledger of 15 AUGUST 2026 records deep disagreement
  about Hick's Real as PROMOTED TO CORE. It is not optional enrichment.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/31: Toleration - three components and one paradox

```ascii-master
WHAT TOLERATION IS - three conditions, ALL required
  (i)   OBJECTION   I genuinely disapprove of, or believe false, what is tolerated
  (ii)  ACCEPTANCE  I have reasons that OUTWEIGH but do not CANCEL the objection
  (iii) POWER       I COULD interfere, and deliberately REFRAIN
  remove (i)   ==> INDIFFERENCE or APPROVAL, not toleration
  remove (iii) ==> MERE ENDURANCE, not toleration
  ==> TOLERATION IS NOT INDIFFERENCE.
THE PARADOX OF TOLERATION
  to tolerate is to ALLOW WHAT ONE HOLDS WRONG
     -> looks WEAK-WILLED  if the objection is serious
     -> looks INSINCERE    if it is not
RESOLUTION BY LEVELS
  FIRST-ORDER   : your doctrine is false
  SECOND-ORDER  : coercing belief is wrong, ineffective, or destructive of the very good
                  at stake
  ==> the reasons for ACCEPTANCE sit at a DIFFERENT LEVEL from the objection.
WHY THE DEFINITION DOES THE WORK: the 2020 stem asks for the IMPORTANCE of tolerance and says
  "justify". Define it by the three components first, and the importance FOLLOWS from the
  definition instead of having to be asserted.
CONSEQUENCE OF (i): the objection component is what makes toleration a MORAL ACHIEVEMENT
  rather than mere indifference - so a society of genuine disagreement cannot be built on
  esteem alone.
CONSEQUENCE OF (iii): toleration is always exercised by someone who COULD HAVE DONE OTHERWISE
  - which is why tolerance from the strong to the weak is PERMISSION, NOT EQUALITY (Panel 22).
TRAP: do not treat "tolerance" as one thing. Grade it (Panel 22) before praising any example.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/31: Forst's ladder and the Western argument-stock

```ascii-master
FORST, TOLERATION IN CONFLICT - four conceptions. GRADE EVERY CLAIM ON THIS LADDER.
  4 ESTEEM       positively valuing the other's beliefs as containing something worthwhile
  3 RESPECT      reciprocal recognition as equal moral-political persons DESPITE deep
                 ethical disagreement
  ------------   ONLY 3 AND 4 ARE COMPATIBLE WITH EQUAL CITIZENSHIP
  2 COEXISTENCE  mutual, pragmatic modus vivendi to avoid conflict
  1 PERMISSION   the powerful permit a minority to exist on sufferance
                 ==> MOST HISTORICAL "TOLERANCE" WAS THIS
THE WESTERN ARGUMENT-STOCK
  LOCKE, A Letter Concerning Toleration (1689) - THREE arguments
     (a) the magistrate has NO COMMISSION over souls
     (b) FORCE CANNOT PRODUCE BELIEF, only profession
     (c) even if it could, coerced conformity WOULD NOT SAVE
     ==> (b) is a claim about the PSYCHOLOGY OF BELIEF, so it is available to an EXCLUSIVIST
  MILL, On Liberty (1859)
     (a) the HARM PRINCIPLE
     (b) the EPISTEMIC argument: suppressing an opinion robs us of either the truth or
         "the clearer perception of truth produced by its collision with error"
  POPPER, The Open Society and Its Enemies, note to ch. 7 - THE PARADOX OF TOLERANCE
     unlimited tolerance leads to the disappearance of tolerance, so a tolerant society must
     retain the right to refuse to tolerate the intolerant
     ==> POPPER'S OWN FORMULATION RESERVES THIS FOR THOSE WHO REFUSE RATIONAL ARGUMENT AND
         ANSWER WITH VIOLENCE. Do NOT state it as a general licence to suppress.
  RAWLS - an OVERLAPPING CONSENSUS on political principles allows citizens with incompatible
     comprehensive doctrines to share a stable polity.
LIMITS MUST BE DRAWN BY HARM AND RECIPROCITY, NOT BY DOCTRINAL CONTENT - otherwise the
  limit-drawing becomes the intolerance it was meant to prevent.
OBJECTION: toleration is second-best; respect and esteem are what a plural society needs, and
  toleration keeps the tolerator in the position of JUDGE.
REPLY: correct as a critique of LEVEL 1 - but the objection component is what makes toleration
  a moral achievement, and a society of genuine disagreement cannot be built on esteem alone.
```

```ascii-master
  "Toleration is INDISPENSABLE BUT INSUFFICIENT. Its indispensability lies in the OBJECTION
   COMPONENT - it is precisely tolerance OF WHAT ONE THINKS WRONG that a plural society
   requires. Its insufficiency lies in the POWER COMPONENT: a tolerance granted by the strong
   to the weak is PERMISSION, NOT EQUALITY, and only respect-based toleration is compatible
   with equal citizenship."

  analysing the concept, the other by refusing the condescension it carries.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 24/31: Religious freedom - the bundle and its four justifications

```ascii-master
THE BUNDLE - different members, different protection
  CONSCIENCE (the inner forum) ------------------------------- ABSOLUTELY PROTECTED
  PROFESS   (declare)      \
  PRACTISE  (act)           >-- LIMITABLE by PUBLIC ORDER, MORALITY and HEALTH
  PROPAGATE (persuade)     /
  CHANGE OR LEAVE a religion ------------------------------- follows from conscience
  FREEDOM FROM compelled religious observance
  ASSOCIATIONAL right of communities to order their internal affairs
  ==> "possible?" must be answered DIFFERENTLY for different members of the bundle.
WHY IT IS PHILOSOPHICALLY DEFENSIBLE - four INDEPENDENT arguments
  1 LOCKE'S INCOERCIBILITY  belief is not directly voluntary, so coercion produces
                            HYPOCRISY, not faith
  2 AUTONOMY                matters of ultimate concern are the PARADIGM CASE in which a
                            person must judge FOR HERSELF
  3 EPISTEMIC               given deep disagreement and fallibility, NO INSTITUTION is
                            entitled to the certainty that suppression presupposes
  4 SOCIAL                  in a plural society, enforced uniformity is a CAUSE of conflict
                            rather than its cure
OBJECTION: religious freedom is a LIBERAL value being universalised, and traditions that
  reject liberal individualism have not consented to it.
REPLY: the incoercibility argument is NOT DISTINCTIVELY LIBERAL - it is a claim about the
  PSYCHOLOGY OF BELIEF - and traditions from Asoka's edicts to Sufi and Bhakti universalism
  reach comparable conclusions ON THEIR OWN GROUNDS.
SECOND OBJECTION: unlimited freedom of practice makes governance impossible.
REPLY: the limits are REAL; the philosophical task is to justify them by HARM AND RECIPROCITY
  rather than by ORTHODOXY.
OPENING LINE: "Religious freedom is possible in a multireligious society, and is more nearly
  NECESSARY there than in a homogeneous one. What is not possible is an UNLIMITED or a
  PERFECTLY NEUTRAL version of it."
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 25/31: Religious freedom - the five hard problems

```ascii-master
  1 PRACTICE / BELIEF ASYMMETRY
      belief ABSOLUTELY protected; PRACTICE is not, because it can HARM
      every constitutional order limits practice by PUBLIC ORDER, MORALITY and HEALTH
      DIFFICULTY: those limits are stated in terms whose CONTENT IS CONTESTED
  2 PROPAGATION AND CONVERSION
      if profession is free, propagation FOLLOWS
      but conversion is experienced by communities as LOSS, and "inducement" is hard to
      distinguish from "persuasion"
      THE PRINCIPLED LINE: COERCION AND FRAUD - not persuasion as such
  3 INTERNAL MINORITIES - AYELET SHACHAR'S "PARADOX OF MULTICULTURAL VULNERABILITY"
      group rights granted to protect a community FROM the majority can ENTRENCH the power of
      that community's DOMINANT members over its own WEAKER members, typically women
      accommodating a GROUP may thus REDUCE the freedom of PERSONS INSIDE IT
      ==> THE SINGLE MOST EXAMINABLE TENSION IN THE CLAUSE
  4 STATE NEUTRALITY - two models
      WALL OF SEPARATION      the state abstains entirely
      PRINCIPLED DISTANCE     RAJEEV BHARGAVA - the Indian model: the state may engage with or
      (the Indian model)      abstain from religions DIFFERENTIALLY, guided by FREEDOM and
                              EQUALITY rather than by a uniform rule of non-engagement
      ==> principled distance PERMITS REFORM (temple-entry, abolition of untouchability)
          that strict separation would FORBID
  5 JUDICIAL DETERMINATION OF WHAT COUNTS AS RELIGION
      the "essential religious practices" doctrine makes the state an ARBITER OF THEOLOGY
      philosophically this sits uneasily with neutrality, whichever way particular cases go
      ==> NOTE THE TENSION; DO NOT LITIGATE CASES
VERDICT FORMULA: "Religious freedom is POSSIBLE in a multireligious society, and is more nearly
  NECESSARY there. What is not possible is an unlimited or perfectly neutral version: every
  regime must limit practice, and every limit embodies a judgement. The defensible position is
  a PRINCIPLED DISTANCE justified by freedom and equality, with the INTERNAL-MINORITIES problem
  treated as A STANDING COST, NOT A SOLVED ONE."
TRAP: do not answer with a list of constitutional articles. The philosophy is the asymmetry,
  the conversion line, the internal minorities, the neutrality models and the arbiter problem.
```

```ascii-master
  (c) religion is THE DEFINING ENGINE of human conflict  FALSE

  authority is IN PRINCIPLE a conflict-generating combination:
     what is at stake is held INFINITE
     dissent is HERESY

     2 blind obedience
     3 establishing the "ideal" time
     4 the end justifying any means

  ==> A TYPOLOGY, NOT A LAW. Say so.

  version (c) requires "religion" to be an ISOLABLE INDEPENDENT VARIABLE across all periods
  and cultures - and Panel 27 shows that assumption is exactly what is contested.

  catalogue of wars. A list of conflicts answers a DIFFERENT question and cannot be graded
  above the average band.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 27/31: Conflict history - the case against, the four roles, the evidence

```ascii-master
THE CASE AGAINST
  WILLIAM CAVANAUGH, The Myth of Religious Violence (2009)
     the very distinction between "religious" and "secular" violence is a MODERN WESTERN
     CONSTRUCTION; there is no trans-historical ESSENCE "religion" that can be isolated as a
     variable; and the myth FUNCTIONS to license the secular state's own violence as rational
  KAREN ARMSTRONG, Fields of Blood (2014)
     PRE-MODERN RELIGION WAS NOT A SEPARABLE SPHERE at all, so labelling agrarian-state
     warfare "religious" is ANACHRONISTIC
  AMARTYA SEN, Identity and Violence (2006)
     THE SINGULAR-AFFILIATION FALLACY - reducing persons to ONE identity among the many they
     actually have - is what turns identity LETHAL
THE ANALYTIC MOVE THAT EARNS MARKS - FOUR ROLES
  +--------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
  | CAUSE        | religion GENERATES the conflict                       (rare)  |
  | MARKER       | religion LABELS an antagonism generated by land, power,       |
  |              | economics                                                     |
  | MOBILISER    | religion RECRUITS for a pre-existing grievance                 |
  | LEGITIMISER  | religion SANCTIFIES a decision taken on other grounds          |
  +--------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
  MOST HISTORICAL CASES ARE THE LAST THREE. A claim of type CAUSE must be argued CASE BY CASE.
THE COUNTER-EVIDENCE (a history that counts only the wars is a SELECTED history)
  Asoka's dhamma policy after KALINGA | monastic preservation of learning | hospitals and
  famine relief | abolitionism | the American civil-rights movement | Gandhi's satyagraha |
  the Bhakti and Sufi traditions' social universalism | the modern interfaith movement
SYMMETRY OBJECTION (concede it in one clause): if "religion" cannot be isolated as a variable,
  it also cannot be CREDITED with the charity and reform above. CORRECT CONCLUSION: the
  deconstruction weakens BOTH the indictment AND the defence, and what survives on both sides
  is the narrower, testable claim about SPECIFIC MECHANISMS.
VERDICT FORMULA: "The claim is HISTORICALLY SELECTIVE and CONCEPTUALLY CONFUSED. Selective,
  because it counts conflict and not the reconciliation, charity and reform the same traditions
  produced; confused, because 'religion' is not a stable independent variable separable from
  politics, land and identity. What is true is NARROWER and worth conceding: EXCLUSIVIST
  CERTAINTY JOINED TO SINGULAR IDENTITY IS DANGEROUS, and religions supply both more readily
  than most other institutions."
```

```ascii-master
  * "Pluralism need not deny absolute reality; it denies EXHAUSTIVE POSSESSION of it by a

  * "Hard contradiction must be ROUTED, not rhetorically harmonised."
  * "Toleration granted by the strong to the weak is PERMISSION, NOT EQUALITY."
  * "Religion is a HIGH-AMPLITUDE social force whose direction is set by the scope of its
     ethics and the politics of its identity."
  * "Unresolvability is EPISTEMIC, NOT ALETHIC - deep disagreement explains persistence
     without vindicating any party."
THE QUALIFIED CONCLUSION, WITH ITS DEFEATER
  DEFENSIBLE: no finite tradition exhaustively possesses the absolute; hard contradictions must
    be acknowledged rather than harmonised; deep disagreement makes the procedural virtues
    OBLIGATORY. That supports pluralism as an ETHIC OF ENGAGEMENT.
  THE DEFEATER THAT MUST BE CARRIED: D'Costa's charge that the pluralist's own second-order
    account is itself a privileged framework. NO POSITION IN THIS CLAUSE IS NEUTRAL - which is
    why the final question is not WHO IS NEUTRAL but WHOSE COMMITMENT IS HELD CORRIGIBLY.
```
